- **Rotavirus causes severe dehydrating diarrhoea in children under 5, with a WINTER seasonality in temperate areas; the vaccine has substantially reduced hospital admissions and deaths.**
- **The wider live-vaccine rules are examinable: LIVE vaccines (BCG, OPV, measles/MMR, rotavirus, varicella, yellow fever, typhoid Ty21a, JE SA 14-14-2) are CONTRAINDICATED IN PREGNANCY and in SEVERE IMMUNODEFICIENCY; if two live vaccines are not given on the SAME DAY, they must be separated by AT LEAST 4 WEEKS. BCG and OPV are safely given together at birth.**
- **Management of rotavirus gastroenteritis is ORS AND ZINC (14 days) — antibiotics and antimotility drugs have no role.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "True about rotavirus vaccine EXCEPT" → **not given with OPV** (it can be).

**REMEMBER:** *Start before 15 weeks, finish by 32 — and OPV on the same day is fine.*

`Q11366`

---

## 97. Prozone vs Post-zone

**PEARL:** **Prozone = ANTIBODY excess** — the test is **negative when neat and becomes POSITIVE on dilution**. **Post-zone = ANTIGEN excess** — the test is **positive at first and becomes NEGATIVE on dilution**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Agglutination and precipitation need a **lattice**. That lattice only forms at the **zone of equivalence**. With too much antibody, every antigen site is capped by a separate antibody and no cross-linking occurs; with too much antigen, every antibody is saturated by separate antigens. Either extreme gives a **false negative**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The classic prozone false negative is the VDRL in secondary syphilis** (and in HIV co-infection) — enormous antibody titres. **Always ask the laboratory to dilute the serum** if syphilis is strongly suspected but the VDRL is negative.
- Also seen in **brucella agglutination** tests, and in the **blood bank** (weak reactions from antibody excess).
- The **Heidelberger–Kendall curve** is the graph of this: precipitate rises to a peak at equivalence, then falls on either side.
- Post-zone matters in **early infection or very high antigen load** (e.g. cryptococcal antigen prozone effect).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The vignette gives the direction of change on dilution — **"positive then negative on dilution" = POST-zone; "negative then positive" = PROzone**.

**REMEMBER:** *Prozone = too much antibody (dilute and it appears); post-zone = too much antigen (dilute and it vanishes).*

`Q11346`

---

## 98. Neisseria — LOS, not LPS

**PEARL:** *Neisseria* (meningitidis and gonorrhoeae) has **lipo-OLIGOsaccharide (LOS)** in its outer membrane — it **lacks the long repeating O-antigen side chain** of classical LPS.

**UNDERSTAND:** The short sugar chains can be **decorated with sialic acid that mimics human surface glycolipids** — molecular mimicry that lets the organism evade complement and antibody. It also **varies phase and antigenic type rapidly**, which is a major reason a gonococcal vaccine has been so hard to make.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **LOS endotoxin drives the fulminant picture of meningococcaemia** — shock, DIC and **purpura fulminans**; **Waterhouse–Friderichsen syndrome** is bilateral adrenal haemorrhage.
- **Meningococcus is capsulated (serogroups A, B, C, W, X, Y) — the capsule is the vaccine target**; **gonococcus has NO capsule**, hence no vaccine and no lasting immunity.
- **Terminal complement (C5–C9) deficiency → recurrent Neisserial infections** — a favourite immunology link. **Eculizumab** carries the same risk, so patients must be vaccinated.
- Both are **Gram-negative, kidney/bean-shaped intracellular DIPLOCOCCI, oxidase positive**; **meningococcus ferments maltose AND glucose; gonococcus ferments glucose only** ("**MeninGitis: Maltose and Glucose**").
- Culture on **chocolate agar / Thayer–Martin (VCN) medium**, in 5–10% CO2.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** An image or description of the cell wall → identify the organism by **LOS**.

**REMEMBER:** *Neisseria's endotoxin is short (LOS) — but it is the one that kills fastest.*

`Q11341`

---

## 99. Sporotrichosis

**PEARL:** ***Sporothrix schenckii*** — a **DIMORPHIC fungus** causing **subcutaneous** infection with **nodules and ulcers spreading in a line along the LYMPHATICS** ("**rose gardener's disease**", after a thorn prick).

**UNDERSTAND:** **Dimorphic = mould in the environment (25°C), yeast in tissue (37°C).** It enters through minor trauma from vegetation, then travels proximally up the lymphatics, producing the pathognomonic **chain of nodules — "sporotrichoid spread"**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Dimorphic fungi:** ***B**lastomyces, **C**occidioides, **P**aracoccidioides, **H**istoplasma, **S**porothrix, **T**alaromyces (Penicillium) marneffei* — "**Bring Cold Pizza Home, So Tasty**".
- Tissue form: **cigar-shaped yeasts**; the classical (rarely seen) histology is the **asteroid body** (yeast with radiating eosinophilic material).
- **Treatment: itraconazole** (potassium iodide is the cheap classical alternative); **amphotericin B** for disseminated or pulmonary disease.
- **Differential for sporotrichoid (nodular lymphangitic) spread:** ***Mycobacterium marinum*** (fish tank/swimming pool granuloma), *Nocardia brasiliensis*, *Leishmania*, and atypical mycobacteria.
- Occupational: gardeners, florists, farmers, and those handling **sphagnum moss and hay**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Dimorphic fungus involving subcutaneous layers" → **Sporothrix** (the distractors chromoblastomycosis and rhinosporidiosis are also subcutaneous but not dimorphic in this sense).

**REMEMBER:** *Thorn prick, then a ladder of nodules climbing the arm — Sporothrix.*

`Q11343`

---

## 100. Influenza Replication

**PEARL:** **Haemagglutinin binds sialic acid → endocytosis → endosomal acidification (via the M2 channel) uncoats the virus → RNA replication in the NUCLEUS → assembly at the membrane → NEURAMINIDASE cleaves sialic acid to release progeny.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Influenza is the great exception — **an RNA virus that replicates in the NUCLEUS** (because it steals 5′ caps from host mRNA, "cap-snatching"). Every antiviral maps onto one step of this cycle, which is why the cycle is worth knowing.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Neuraminidase inhibitors (oseltamivir, zanamivir, peramivir)** block **release**; **amantadine/rimantadine** block the **M2 ion channel** (influenza A only, now largely resistant); **baloxavir** blocks the **cap-dependent endonuclease**.
- **Antigenic DRIFT** = minor point mutations → seasonal epidemics, need for annual vaccine. **Antigenic SHIFT** = **reassortment of segmented genome** → **pandemics** (only influenza A; the segmented genome makes it possible).
- **Pigs are the classic "mixing vessel"** — they carry receptors for both avian and human strains.
- Other RNA viruses replicating in the nucleus: **retroviruses and Borna virus**; the DNA virus exception that replicates in the **cytoplasm** is **poxvirus**.
- **HA mediates attachment and is the main neutralising antibody target; NA mediates release.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** A replication-cycle diagram → identify the virus. Nuclear replication + surface glycoprotein cleavage = influenza.

**REMEMBER:** *HA gets in, NA gets out — and uniquely for an RNA virus, the copying happens in the nucleus.*

`Q11348`

---

## 101. Visceral Leishmaniasis — Diagnosis and Treatment

**PEARL:** ***Leishmania donovani* amastigotes live INSIDE macrophages**, so the **BUFFY COAT** (concentrated leukocytes) is used for peripheral diagnosis. **Splenic aspirate is the most sensitive (>95%)**; **bone marrow is safer**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Because the parasite is **obligate intracellular in macrophages**, you must look where the macrophages are — hence buffy coat, marrow, spleen and liver, not a plain blood smear.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Kala-azar features:** **massive splenomegaly > hepatomegaly, pancytopenia, fever with a double rise, hypergammaglobulinaemia with reversed A:G ratio, and darkening of the skin** ("black fever"). **Post-kala-azar dermal leishmaniasis (PKDL)** appears months–years later and is the **human reservoir** in India.
- **Vector = female PHLEBOTOMUS (sandfly) — in India *P. argentipes***; the Indian form is **anthroponotic (man is the only reservoir)**, which is what makes elimination feasible.
- **Drug of choice in India = LIPOSOMAL AMPHOTERICIN B (single dose)** — sodium **stibogluconate is obsolete in Bihar due to widespread resistance**. **Miltefosine** is the only oral drug (teratogenic — contraindicated in pregnancy).
- **rK39 dipstick** is the field serological test; **aldehyde (Napier's) test** is the old non-specific one.
- **Amastigote = LD body** (intracellular, no flagellum); **promastigote** = flagellated form in the sandfly and in **NNN culture medium**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Bone marrow image with intracellular amastigotes → asked which statement is true. The traps are "obligate **extra**cellular" and "stibogluconate first line in India".

**REMEMBER:** *Inside macrophages, so look in the buffy coat, marrow or spleen — and treat with liposomal amphotericin B.*

`Q11350`

---

## 102. *Clostridioides difficile*

**PEARL:** Diagnose by **TOXIN detection** — **GDH screen + toxin A/B EIA, or NAAT for the toxin gene** — **never by serology/IgM**. Treat with **ORAL fidaxomicin or oral vancomycin**.

**UNDERSTAND:** The disease is **toxin-mediated, not invasive** — so what matters is whether the organism is *producing toxin*, not merely whether it is present. Up to **5% of adults (and most infants) are asymptomatic carriers**, which is why a positive culture or PCR alone does not equal disease.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Oral vancomycin works because it is NOT absorbed** — it stays in the lumen where the organism is. **IV vancomycin is useless** for C. difficile: a favourite trap. (IV **metronidazole** does reach the colon and is used in ileus/fulminant disease.)
- **Toxin A = enterotoxin, Toxin B = cytotoxin** (B is the more important); the **hypervirulent NAP1/BI/027 strain** produces binary toxin and more of both.
- **Pseudomembrane = fibrin, neutrophils and cellular debris** on the mucosa; **"volcano lesions"** histologically.
- **Precipitants:** clindamycin classically, plus **cephalosporins, fluoroquinolones, penicillins**, and **PPIs**.
- **Recurrent disease → FAECAL MICROBIOTA TRANSPLANT** (cure rate ~90%) or **bezlotoxumab**.
- **Infection control: hand-washing with SOAP AND WATER — alcohol rub does NOT kill spores**; contact isolation and sporicidal (hypochlorite) cleaning.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Not true regarding C. difficile" → **"IgM assay confirms the diagnosis"**.

**REMEMBER:** *Toxin-mediated: test the toxin, treat by mouth, and wash with soap — alcohol won't touch the spores.*

`Q11351`

---

## 103. Japanese Encephalitis Cycle

**PEARL:** **PIGS = amplifier host · ARDEID (wading) BIRDS = reservoir · CULEX (tritaeniorhynchus) = vector · HUMANS = dead-end / accidental host.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Humans develop only a **brief, low-level viraemia**, insufficient to infect a feeding mosquito — so **man-to-man transmission does not occur** and humans cannot sustain the cycle. Pigs, by contrast, develop high viraemia without falling ill, hugely amplifying the virus pool near human dwellings.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Control follows directly from the cycle:** **keep piggeries away from human habitation (≥4–5 km)**, vector control of Culex (which breeds in **rice fields and clean stagnant water**), and **vaccination** — the JE vaccine is in India's **Universal Immunisation Programme** in endemic districts.
- **JE is the leading cause of viral encephalitis in Asia**; a **Flavivirus** (single-stranded RNA), related to dengue, West Nile, yellow fever and Zika.
- Clinically: fever, altered sensorium, **seizures**, and characteristically **parkinsonian features / movement disorders** from **THALAMIC involvement** — MRI shows **bilateral thalamic lesions**, a strong clue.
- **CSF and serum IgM capture ELISA (MAC-ELISA)** is the diagnostic test.
- Most infections are **subclinical** (roughly 1 in 250 develops encephalitis), but case fatality in symptomatic cases is **20–30%** with neurological sequelae in half of survivors.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Matching question: host ↔ role. The one people get wrong is that **birds, not pigs, are the reservoir** — pigs are the *amplifier*.

**REMEMBER:** *Birds keep it, pigs multiply it, Culex carries it, man is a dead end.*

`Q11354`

---

## 104. Fungal Spores and the Arthrospore Clue

**PEARL:** **Hyphae fragmenting into rectangular (barrel-shaped) arthroconidia** in a
farmer with an **upper-lobe cavitary lesion** = **Coccidioides** — arthrospores are the
infective form.

**UNDERSTAND:** Spore morphology is the microbiologist's shorthand for how the fungus
reproduces. **Arthrospores** are formed by the hypha simply breaking apart at septa, so
they look like a row of bricks; **conidia** are budded off the tip of a specialised
conidiophore (Aspergillus, Penicillium); **ascospores** and **basidiospores** are sexual
spores formed inside an ascus or on a basidium. Only fragmentation gives the rectangular
"box-car" appearance.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Coccidioides immitis/posadasii:** dimorphic — **arthroconidia in soil/mould phase (highly infectious, laboratory hazard, handle in BSL-3)**, **spherules packed with endospores in tissue**. Endemic in south-western USA ("San Joaquin Valley fever"); presents with pneumonia, cavitation, **erythema nodosum**, and disseminates in the immunosuppressed and in pregnancy.
- **Geotrichum** and the dermatophytes also form arthrospores; **Malassezia** shows "spaghetti and meatballs".
- **Asexual spore types:** blastoconidia (budding — yeasts), **arthroconidia** (fragmentation), **chlamydoconidia** (thick-walled resting, e.g. *Candida albicans* on cornmeal agar), sporangiospores (inside a sporangium — **Mucor, Rhizopus**), conidia (Aspergillus, Penicillium).
- **Sexual spores:** ascospores (Ascomycetes), basidiospores (Basidiomycetes — **Cryptococcus** is one), zygospores (Zygomycetes).
- Dimorphic fungi mnemonic — **"Mould in the cold, Yeast in the beast"**: Histoplasma, Blastomyces, Coccidioides (spherule, not yeast), Paracoccidioides, Sporothrix, Talaromyces.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The stem describes the microscopic appearance and asks for the
spore **type** or the organism. "Fragmentation of hyphae into rectangular units" is the
give-away phrase for arthrospores.

**REMEMBER:** *Arthro = joints of the hypha snapping into bricks.*

`Q9559`

---

## 105. Tinea Capitis: Kerion, Ectothrix and Endothrix

**PEARL:** **Kerion** (boggy, inflammatory, pus-exuding scalp swelling) in a child with
**farm-animal contact** = **Trichophyton verrucosum** — a **zoophilic** dermatophyte.
**Spores OUTSIDE the hair shaft (ectothrix) = Microsporum** (audouinii, canis); **spores
INSIDE (endothrix) = Trichophyton tonsurans, violaceum**.

**UNDERSTAND:** The severity of inflammation is set by how foreign the fungus is to
humans. **Anthropophilic** species are adapted to human skin and cause quiet, scaly,
chronic lesions; **zoophilic** and **geophilic** species provoke a violent cell-mediated
response, producing kerion and favus. So "contact with animals" in a stem always predicts
a **severely inflammatory** lesion.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Ectothrix (Microsporum audouinii, M. canis, T. verrucosum, T. mentagrophytes)** — **fluoresces green under Wood's lamp**; hair breaks above the scalp.
- **Endothrix (T. tonsurans, T. violaceum, T. schoenleinii)** — **does NOT fluoresce** (except *T. schoenleinii*, dull blue-green); hair breaks at the surface leaving **"black dot" tinea capitis**.
- **Favus** = *T. schoenleinii* — **scutula** (yellow cup-shaped crusts), mousy odour, scarring alopecia.
- **Treatment of tinea capitis is always SYSTEMIC — topicals never work** because the fungus is inside the follicle. **Griseofulvin** is first-line in children (best for Microsporum); **terbinafine** works better for *Trichophyton*. Kerion additionally may need a short course of oral steroid to prevent scarring alopecia; it is **not** a bacterial abscess and must not be incised.
- **Malassezia furfur** causes **pityriasis (tinea) versicolor**, not tinea capitis — a common distractor.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Two axes are tested: the **animal-contact → zoophilic → kerion** link,
and the **Wood's lamp fluorescence → ectothrix → Microsporum** link.

**REMEMBER:** *Microsporum sits outside and glows; Trichophyton hides inside and doesn't.*

`Q9560, Q9563`

---

## 106. Mycetoma: Eumycetoma vs Actinomycetoma

**PEARL:** **Eumycetoma is FUNGAL; actinomycetoma is BACTERIAL** (filamentous
actinomycetes). Both present as a chronic, **painless**, slowly enlarging swelling of the
foot with **multiple discharging sinuses containing grains (granules)**.

**UNDERSTAND:** The word "myceto-" means fungus, but the syndrome has two aetiologies with
identical clinical appearance and completely different treatment — which is exactly why
the exam keeps asking. Grain colour and microscopy of the granule separate them: the
**filaments of actinomycetes are thin (~1 µm, bacterial width) and Gram-positive and
partially acid-fast**; fungal filaments are **broad (2–6 µm) and septate**.

**MUST KNOW:**

| | **Eumycetoma (fungal)** | **Actinomycetoma (bacterial)** |
|---|---|---|
| Organisms | *Madurella mycetomatis*, *Scedosporium* | *Nocardia*, *Actinomadura*, *Streptomyces somaliensis* |
| Grain colour | Usually **black** (Madurella) or white | **Red/pink** (Actinomadura pelletieri), yellow-white (Nocardia) |
| Filament width | **Broad, septate (>2 µm)** | **Thin (<1 µm)**, Gram-positive, modified acid-fast (Nocardia) |
| Progression | Slower, well-demarcated | Faster, more invasive, bone destruction |
| **Treatment** | **Itraconazole/ketoconazole + SURGERY (wide excision, sometimes amputation)** | **Medical cure — co-trimoxazole + amikacin/dapsone (Welsh regimen); no surgery needed** |

- Also called **Madura foot**; commonest in barefoot agricultural workers in the tropical "mycetoma belt"; entry by thorn prick.
- **Painless** despite gross deformity — pain suggests secondary bacterial infection or bone involvement.
- Diagnosis: examine **grains** from the sinus discharge (direct microscopy, culture, histopathology); MRI shows the **"dot-in-circle" sign**.
- Distinguish from **botryomycosis** (grains, but caused by *Staphylococcus aureus*) and cervicofacial **actinomycosis** (*A. israelii*, **sulphur granules**, anaerobic, treat with high-dose penicillin).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The false statement in an "INCORRECT" question is almost always
"Eumycetoma is caused by bacteria." The other high-yield discriminator is **which one
needs surgery — the fungal one.**

**REMEMBER:** *EU = fUngal, needs the knife; Actino = bacteria, cured by drugs.*

`Q9562`

---

## 107. Trematodes: Where the Adult Lives and What You Ate

**PEARL:** **Paragonimus westermani** (lung fluke, from **undercooked crab/crayfish**) is
the fluke whose eggs are found in **BOTH sputum AND stool** — coughed up and swallowed.

**UNDERSTAND:** Every fluke question reduces to two facts: the **food that transmits it**
and the **organ the adult inhabits**, which together determine where the eggs appear.
Paragonimus is the only one that reaches the lung, so it is the only one in sputum;
liver flukes shed into bile → stool only.

**MUST KNOW:**

| Fluke | Source | Adult site | Key point |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Paragonimus westermani** | **Crab, crayfish** | **Lung** | Haemoptysis mimicking **TB**; eggs in **sputum and stool**; treat praziquantel |
| **Clonorchis sinensis / Opisthorchis** | **Raw freshwater fish** | Bile ducts | **Cholangiocarcinoma** |
| **Fasciola hepatica** | **Watercress**, aquatic plants | Bile ducts (sheep liver fluke) | Large operculated egg; **triclabendazole** (not praziquantel) |
| **Fasciolopsis buski** | **Water chestnut/caltrop** | **Small intestine** (largest intestinal fluke, 2–7 cm) | Diarrhoea, malabsorption, oedema |
| **Schistosoma** | **Skin penetration in water** (snail) | Veins | *S. haematobium* → bladder, **squamous cell carcinoma**; *S. mansoni/japonicum* → portal hypertension |

- **Fasciola and Fasciolopsis have almost identical large operculated eggs** — distinguish by the **adult worm morphology** and by the clinical picture (biliary vs intestinal).
- All trematodes use a **snail as the first intermediate host**.
- **Praziquantel** treats essentially all flukes **except Fasciola** (triclabendazole).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The stem gives you the food or the specimen. **Crab → Paragonimus →
sputum + stool. Watercress → Fasciola. Raw fish → Clonorchis → cholangiocarcinoma.**

**REMEMBER:** *Only the lung fluke gets coughed up — so only Paragonimus is in sputum.*

`Q9564, Q9565`

---

## 108. Hepatitis B: A Partially Double-Stranded DNA Virus

**PEARL:** HBV has a **partially double-stranded circular DNA** genome — a complete
negative-sense strand and an incomplete positive strand — and it **replicates through an
RNA intermediate using reverse transcriptase**, uniquely among human DNA viruses.

**UNDERSTAND:** The gapped genome exists because the viral polymerase is a **reverse
transcriptase**: the pregenomic RNA is reverse-transcribed into the minus strand, and
plus-strand synthesis is left incomplete when the particle is packaged. Two consequences
follow directly and are heavily examined: the virus is susceptible to **nucleoside
analogues** (tenofovir, entecavir, lamivudine) that also treat HIV, and it forms
**cccDNA** in the hepatocyte nucleus, which is why HBV is **suppressed but not cured**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Family **Hepadnaviridae**; the intact virion is the **Dane particle**; HBsAg forms the envelope and is produced in vast excess as non-infectious spheres and filaments.
- Serology: **HBsAg** = current infection (>6 months = chronic); **anti-HBs** = immunity (vaccination gives anti-HBs **alone**); **anti-HBc IgM** = the only marker positive in the **window period**; **HBeAg** = high replication and infectivity; **HBV DNA** = the quantitative measure used for treatment decisions.
- The vaccine is **recombinant HBsAg** — a subunit vaccine, safe in pregnancy and immunosuppression.
- Compare genomes: **Parvovirus B19 = the only single-stranded DNA virus; Reovirus/Rotavirus = double-stranded RNA; Poxvirus = the largest, replicates in the cytoplasm; Retroviruses = ssRNA with reverse transcriptase.**
- HBV integrates into the host genome → **hepatocellular carcinoma even without cirrhosis** (unlike HCV).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** A one-line genome-identification question; the trap is "partially
**single**-stranded" — the convention names it by the strand that is complete, so it is
**partially double-stranded**.

**REMEMBER:** *A DNA virus that reverse-transcribes, with one strand left unfinished.*

`Q9566`

---

## 109. Brucella Species and Their Reservoirs

**PEARL:** **B. melitensis — GOATS and sheep** (the most virulent and commonest human
pathogen); **B. abortus — cattle; B. suis — pigs; B. canis — dogs.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Brucella is a **facultative intracellular Gram-negative coccobacillus**
that survives inside macrophages, which explains everything about the disease: an
**undulant, relapsing fever**, **granuloma** formation in the reticuloendothelial system,
the need for **prolonged combination therapy with intracellularly active drugs**, and the
need for **prolonged culture**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Transmission: **unpasteurised milk and soft cheese** (commonest), direct contact with animal tissue/placenta (occupational — vets, abattoir workers, farmers), and **laboratory aerosols — Brucella is one of the commonest laboratory-acquired infections** (handle in BSL-3, alert the lab).
- Clinical: undulant fever, drenching **malodorous sweat**, arthralgia, **sacroiliitis and spondylitis** (commonest focal form), hepatosplenomegaly, orchitis, and rarely endocarditis (the main cause of death).
- Diagnosis: **blood/bone marrow culture** (bone marrow is more sensitive; incubate up to 4 weeks — **Castaneda biphasic medium**), **standard agglutination test titre ≥1:160**, and PCR. **B. abortus needs 5–10% CO₂**; H₂S is produced by *abortus* and *suis*, not *melitensis*.
- **Treatment: doxycycline 6 weeks + streptomycin/gentamicin, or doxycycline + rifampicin.** In children <8 years and pregnancy: **co-trimoxazole + rifampicin**. Monotherapy relapses.
- **There is no human vaccine**; control is by animal vaccination and pasteurisation.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Species-to-animal matching, or a stem about a **farmer/veterinarian
with undulant fever and back pain**. Goat = melitensis is the single most asked pair.

**REMEMBER:** *MELitensis for goats and MILk — the meanest of them all.*

`Q9567`

---

## 110. Rocky Mountain Spotted Fever and the Direction of Rashes

**PEARL:** **RMSF (Rickettsia rickettsii, tick-borne)** — fever and headache after
outdoor/camping exposure with a rash that begins on the **wrists and ankles** and spreads
**centripetally** (inward to the trunk), **involving palms and soles** and **sparing the
face**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Rickettsiae infect **vascular endothelium**, so the rash is a
**vasculitis**, not an epidermal eruption — it begins maculopapular and becomes
**petechial** as vessels leak, and the same endothelial damage causes the hypotension,
oedema and end-organ failure of severe disease. That is why doxycycline must be started
**on clinical suspicion, before serology confirms**: mortality rises steeply with delay.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Direction of spread is the classic discriminator:**
  - **RMSF** — peripheral → central (**centripetal**), **palms and soles involved**.
  - **Chickenpox** — central → peripheral (**centrifugal**), lesions of **different ages** together, spares palms and soles.
  - **Measles** — begins **behind the ears/hairline and spreads downward**, preceded by the **3 Cs (cough, coryza, conjunctivitis) and Koplik spots**.
  - **Secondary syphilis** — diffuse, **includes palms and soles**, non-itchy, with condylomata lata — the main mimic of RMSF; the discriminator is exposure history and the acute toxicity of RMSF.
  - **Lyme disease** — a single expanding **erythema migrans** target lesion, not a generalised rash.
- **Treatment: DOXYCYCLINE at every age — including children under 8 and in pregnancy** (the tooth-staining risk is negligible for a short course and RMSF is lethal). Chloramphenicol is the alternative in true allergy.
- **Weil–Felix** (OX-19, OX-2 positive for spotted fever group) is an obsolete but still-examined serological test; **indirect immunofluorescence** is the current standard.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The stem always tells you the direction of spread and whether palms
and soles are involved. **Palms + soles = RMSF or syphilis (or hand-foot-and-mouth).**

**REMEMBER:** *Ticks bite the limbs — so the spotted fever rash starts at the wrists and
travels in.*

`Q9568`

---

## 111. Treponema pallidum and the Hard Chancre

**PEARL:** A **painless, indurated, clean-based genital ulcer** 2–10 weeks after exposure
is the **hard chancre of primary syphilis**. On **dark-ground microscopy** *Treponema
pallidum* shows **CORKSCREW motility** — rotating about its long axis while moving
backwards and forwards, with secondary curves appearing and disappearing.

**UNDERSTAND:** The motility is generated by **endoflagella (axial filaments) running
within the periplasmic space** rather than outside the cell, so the whole spiral body
rotates like a corkscrew instead of being pushed by external flagella. The organism is
too slender to be seen on Gram stain and cannot be cultured on artificial media — which
is exactly why **dark-ground/phase-contrast microscopy** of the exudate is the classic
demonstration.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Motility patterns to keep separate:** **corkscrew — Treponema**; **darting/shooting-star — Vibrio cholerae**; **tumbling — Listeria** (and end-over-end at 25 °C); **swarming — Proteus**; **gliding — Mycoplasma/Capnocytophaga**; **falling-leaf — Giardia**; **jerky — Trichomonas**.
- **Genital ulcer differential:** **syphilis — painless, indurated, clean, non-tender nodes**; **chancroid (H. ducreyi) — PAINFUL, ragged, undermined, soft, tender suppurative nodes, "school of fish" on Gram stain**; **LGV (C. trachomatis L1–3) — transient ulcer with painful groove-sign buboes**; **granuloma inguinale (Klebsiella granulomatis) — beefy-red, Donovan bodies**; **HSV — multiple painful vesicles/erosions**.
- **Serology: non-treponemal (VDRL, RPR)** — cheap, quantitative, used for **screening and to monitor treatment response** (titre falls); biological false positives in pregnancy, SLE, antiphospholipid syndrome, EBV, malaria, leprosy. **Treponemal (TPHA, FTA-ABS, TPPA)** — confirmatory, **stay positive for life**.
- **Prozone phenomenon** — a falsely negative VDRL in secondary syphilis from very high antibody titres; dilute the serum.
- **Treatment: single dose benzathine penicillin G 2.4 MU IM** for early syphilis (3 weekly doses for late latent); **penicillin is the only proven therapy in pregnancy — desensitise if allergic**. Watch for the **Jarisch–Herxheimer reaction** in the first 24 hours.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Dark-field image or ulcer description → the question is usually the
**motility type** or the **confirmatory test**. Painless = syphilis; painful = chancroid.

**REMEMBER:** *Painless and hard, corkscrewing in the dark.*

`Q10836`

---

## 112. Selective Culture Media

**PEARL:** **Ludlam's medium (lithium chloride + tellurite)** is the selective medium for
**Staphylococcus aureus** when it is scanty or outnumbered by other flora.

**UNDERSTAND:** A **selective** medium contains an agent that inhibits the unwanted flora
while the target organism tolerates it — here, high salt/lithium and tellurite suppress
competing bacteria while staphylococci grow (and reduce tellurite to black colonies). A
**differential** medium instead lets everything grow but makes the target look different
(MacConkey, where lactose fermenters turn pink). Many media are both.

**MUST KNOW:**

| Organism | Selective medium |
|---|---|
| **Staph. aureus** | **Ludlam's**, salt–milk agar, mannitol salt agar, Roberts' cooked meat with 10% NaCl |
| **Strep. pyogenes** | **Pike's medium (transport)**, crystal violet blood agar |
| **Neisseria gonorrhoeae/meningitidis** | **Thayer–Martin / modified New York City** (VCN — vancomycin, colistin, nystatin) |
| **Corynebacterium diphtheriae** | **Loeffler's serum slope** (enrichment), **potassium tellurite (McLeod's)** — black colonies |
| **Vibrio cholerae** | **TCBS** (yellow colonies), alkaline peptone water (enrichment), **Venkatraman–Ramakrishnan (transport)** |
| **Salmonella/Shigella** | **DCA, XLD, Wilson–Blair (bismuth sulphite)**; **selenite F / tetrathionate broth** (enrichment) |
| **Mycobacterium tuberculosis** | **Lowenstein–Jensen** (malachite green as the selective agent) |
| **Bordetella pertussis** | **Bordet–Gengou**, Regan–Lowe |
| **Legionella** | **BCYE** (buffered charcoal yeast extract) |
| **Campylobacter** | **Skirrow's**, Butzler |
| **Fungi** | **Sabouraud dextrose agar** (with chloramphenicol/cycloheximide) |

- **Transport media** (no growth, just survival): **Stuart's, Amies, Cary–Blair** (enteric pathogens), **Pike's** (streptococci), **VR fluid** (cholera).
- **Enrichment media are liquid** and favour the pathogen's multiplication (selenite F, alkaline peptone water); **enriched media** are solid with added nutrients (blood agar, chocolate agar).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Straight matching. The perennial traps are Thayer–Martin (Neisseria,
not Staph) and Pike's (a **transport** medium for Streptococcus).

**REMEMBER:** *Ludlam's for the staph, Thayer–Martin for the gonococcus, Pike's just
carries the strep.*

`Q10847`

---

## 113. Neisseria gonorrhoeae

**PEARL:** Urethral discharge with **Gram-negative diplococci INSIDE polymorphs** =
*Neisseria gonorrhoeae*. It is **oxidase and catalase positive**, uses **pili** as its key
virulence and antigenic-variation factor, and is **facultatively intracellular** — but it
is **NON-MOTILE in the classical sense; it does not show "twitching motility"** as the
examined answer.

**UNDERSTAND:** Gonococcus has no flagella; its surface pili mediate **attachment to
urethral and cervical epithelium and phase/antigenic variation**, which is why natural
infection confers **no protective immunity and reinfection is common** — and why there is
still no vaccine. Its survival inside neutrophils is what makes the intracellular
diplococci on the smear diagnostic in men.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Diagnosis:** Gram stain of urethral discharge is ~95% sensitive **in symptomatic men** but poor in women (cervical flora) → use **NAAT (PCR)**, the test of choice for all sites. Culture on **Thayer–Martin/modified New York City (VCN)** medium in 5–10% CO₂ retains a role for **antibiotic susceptibility**.
- **Treatment: single-dose ceftriaxone 500 mg IM** (dose raised because of resistance), **plus doxycycline 100 mg BD for 7 days for co-existing chlamydia** unless excluded. Quinolones and penicillins are no longer used empirically — gonococcus is a WHO priority resistant pathogen. **Treat the partner; screen for other STIs including HIV and syphilis.**
- **Complications:** **PID, tubal infertility, ectopic pregnancy, Fitz-Hugh–Curtis syndrome** (perihepatitis, "violin-string" adhesions); in men, epididymo-orchitis and urethral stricture.
- **Disseminated gonococcal infection:** **triad of migratory polyarthralgia, tenosynovitis and pustular skin lesions**, or a purulent monoarthritis — associated with **terminal complement (C5–C9) deficiency**, which also predisposes to recurrent meningococcal disease.
- **Ophthalmia neonatorum:** gonococcal presents at **days 2–5** (hyperacute, copious purulent, corneal perforation risk — treat systemic ceftriaxone); **chlamydial at days 5–14** (treat **oral erythromycin**, not just topicals, to clear nasopharyngeal carriage and prevent pneumonia).
- **Twitching motility** is a genuine pilus-mediated phenomenon in **Pseudomonas**, *Moraxella* and some *Neisseria* under laboratory conditions — the exam treats it as the false statement here, so answer to the question's convention.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Gram-stain image plus "all are true EXCEPT". Know the true triad —
**pili, intracellular, oxidase + catalase positive**.

**REMEMBER:** *Gram-negative diplococci inside the pus cell — oxidase positive, pilus
powered.*

`Q10881`

---

## 114. Plasmodium falciparum on the Blood Film

**PEARL:** Falciparum is recognised by **multiple ring forms (often 2 or more) per red
cell, delicate double-chromatin-dot "headphone" rings, APPLIQUÉ/accolé forms at the cell
margin, high parasitaemia, normal-sized red cells, and BANANA-SHAPED (crescentic)
gametocytes**. **Only rings and gametocytes are seen** — the other stages are hidden.

**UNDERSTAND:** The missing stages are the key to the disease. Falciparum-infected red
cells express **PfEMP-1**, which binds endothelial receptors (**CD36, ICAM-1, chondroitin
sulphate A in the placenta**) — so the maturing trophozoites and schizonts **sequester in
the capillaries of the brain, gut and placenta** and never circulate. That sequestration
is simultaneously why the smear shows only rings **and** why falciparum alone causes
**cerebral malaria, placental malaria and multi-organ failure**.

**MUST KNOW:**

| | **P. falciparum** | **P. vivax / ovale** |
|---|---|---|
| RBC size | **Normal** | **Enlarged**, Schüffner dots |
| Stages on smear | **Rings + banana gametocytes only** | **All stages**, amoeboid trophozoites |
| Parasitaemia | **High, multiple rings/cell** | Low, one ring/cell |
| Cell preference | **All ages of RBC** | **Reticulocytes** (vivax), Duffy antigen required |
| Relapse | **No hypnozoites** | **Hypnozoites → relapse** |
| Severity | **Cerebral malaria, blackwater fever, ARDS, AKI** | Benign tertian; vivax can still be severe |

- **P. malariae** — band-form trophozoites, rosette schizonts, **quartan** fever, **nephrotic syndrome**. **P. knowlesi** — mimics malariae morphologically but causes **daily fever and severe disease** (macaque reservoir, Southeast Asia).
- **Severe falciparum: IV ARTESUNATE** (superior to quinine in mortality — SEAQUAMAT/AQUAMAT), then a full oral ACT course. Uncomplicated falciparum in India: **artesunate + sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine (or ACT) + a single dose of primaquine 0.75 mg/kg as a gametocytocide**.
- **Vivax: chloroquine (still effective in India) for the blood stage + PRIMAQUINE 14 days for radical cure** of hypnozoites — **check G6PD first**, and **primaquine is contraindicated in pregnancy** (use chloroquine prophylaxis until delivery).
- **Chloroquine is the treatment of choice for vivax malaria in pregnancy** and is safe throughout.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The smear image — count the rings per cell and look for **banana
gametocytes** or **appliqué forms**. Enlarged red cells with Schüffner dots means vivax.

**REMEMBER:** *Only rings and bananas circulate — everything else is stuck to the
endothelium.*

`Q10872, Q9580`

---

## 115. Ebola Virus Disease

**PEARL:** **Ebola** — a **filovirus (filamentous, "shepherd's crook", enveloped, negative-
sense single-stranded RNA)** whose natural reservoir is the **FRUIT BAT**, with primates
as amplifying hosts and **human-to-human spread by direct contact with body fluids** (not
airborne).

**UNDERSTAND:** The transmission route dictates the entire public health response. Because
spread requires **contact with blood, vomit, stool or a corpse**, the highest-risk
activities are **caring for the sick and traditional burial practices** — which is why
outbreak control rests on **isolation, contact tracing, safe and dignified burial and
barrier nursing (PPE)** rather than on respiratory measures. Infectivity rises with viral
load, so patients are most contagious when **sickest and after death**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Incubation **2–21 days**; abrupt fever, myalgia, headache → profuse **vomiting and diarrhoea with massive fluid loss** → haemorrhage, shock and multi-organ failure. **Death is largely from hypovolaemia and electrolyte loss**, so **aggressive fluid and electrolyte support** is the mainstay and dramatically lowers mortality.
- Diagnosis: **RT-PCR** on blood (may be negative in the first 72 hours — repeat), antigen ELISA; handle specimens at **BSL-4**.
- **Vaccine: rVSV-ZEBOV (Ervebo)**, used in **ring vaccination** of contacts and health workers. **Monoclonal antibodies (inmazeb, ansuvimab)** improve survival.
- **Convalescence:** the virus persists in **immune-privileged sites — semen (months), ocular fluid, CSF, breast milk** → late sexual transmission and **uveitis**; survivors need counselling and semen testing.
- Other viral haemorrhagic fevers: **Marburg** (also filovirus, bats), **Lassa** (rodent *Mastomys*, West Africa, **ribavirin** works), **CCHF** (**tick-borne, Hyalomma**, nosocomial risk, ribavirin), **Dengue/Yellow fever** (mosquito), **Hantavirus** (rodent excreta).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** A life-cycle diagram with a **bat and primates** is the recognition
cue; the follow-up asks about **transmission (contact, not airborne)** or the **reservoir
(fruit bat)**.

**REMEMBER:** *Bats to apes to people, by touch — and the fluids stay infectious after
death.*

`Q10883`

---

## 116. Identifying Gram-Positive Cocci in Chains

**PEARL:** **Catalase** separates staphylococci (**positive**) from streptococci
(**negative**). Among **β-haemolytic** streptococci, **BACITRACIN sensitivity identifies
Group A (S. pyogenes)**; Group B is **bacitracin resistant**. **Optochin sensitivity and
bile solubility** separate **pneumococcus (sensitive/soluble)** from **viridans
streptococci (resistant/insoluble)** — both **α-haemolytic**.

**UNDERSTAND:** The identification algorithm follows the colony's own appearance. Chains of
Gram-positive cocci → streptococcus (confirm by catalase negativity). Then the
**haemolysis pattern chooses which second test applies**: **β-haemolysis (complete,
clear)** → bacitracin/Lancefield grouping; **α-haemolysis (green, partial)** →
optochin/bile solubility. Applying optochin to a β-haemolytic colony is a category error —
which is exactly the trap in this question.

**MUST KNOW:**

| Haemolysis | Organisms | Distinguishing test |
|---|---|---|
| **β (complete)** | **S. pyogenes (Group A)**, S. agalactiae (Group B) | **Bacitracin: A sensitive, B resistant**; **CAMP test and hippurate: B positive**; **PYR: A positive** |
| **α (partial, green)** | **S. pneumoniae**, viridans group | **Optochin and bile solubility: pneumococcus sensitive/soluble**; viridans resistant/insoluble |
| **γ (none)** | Enterococcus, Group D | **Bile-esculin positive; enterococcus grows in 6.5% NaCl** |

- **S. pyogenes:** pharyngitis, impetigo, erysipelas, cellulitis, scarlet fever, necrotising fasciitis, toxic shock. **Non-suppurative sequelae — rheumatic fever (follows PHARYNGITIS only) and post-streptococcal glomerulonephritis (follows skin OR throat infection)**. **ASO titre** rises after pharyngitis but poorly after skin infection — use **anti-DNase B** for pyoderma.
- **S. agalactiae (Group B):** neonatal sepsis and meningitis — screen pregnant women at **35–37 weeks** and give **intrapartum penicillin**.
- **S. pneumoniae:** lancet-shaped diplococci, **capsule is the main virulence factor** (quellung reaction), commonest cause of community-acquired pneumonia, otitis media and adult meningitis; overwhelming sepsis in **asplenia/sickle cell** → vaccinate.
- **Staph. aureus:** catalase **positive**, **coagulase positive**, golden colonies, **mannitol fermenting** on MSA, **DNase positive**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Read the haemolysis and morphology first — they select the test. Chains
+ β-haemolysis → **bacitracin**.

**REMEMBER:** *Catalase splits staph from strep; bacitracin splits A from B; optochin splits
pneumococcus from viridans.*

`Q10937`

---

## 117. Trichuris trichiura and the Soil-Transmitted Helminths

**PEARL:** **Trichuris trichiura (whipworm)** has a **barrel-shaped egg with bipolar mucus
plugs** and is transmitted by **ingestion of embryonated eggs** in contaminated food or
water — **not** by larval skin penetration.

**UNDERSTAND:** Transmission route follows the **infective stage**, and the infective stage
follows the life cycle's requirement for soil maturation. *Trichuris* and *Ascaris* eggs
must **embryonate in soil for 2–4 weeks** and are then swallowed. **Hookworm and
Strongyloides**, by contrast, hatch in soil and mature to **filariform larvae that
penetrate intact skin** — which is why they cause "ground itch" and are diseases of
barefoot walking rather than of contaminated water.

**MUST KNOW:**

| Worm | Egg/appearance | Transmission | Key disease |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Trichuris trichiura** | **Barrel with bipolar plugs** | **Ingested egg** | **Rectal prolapse** and dysentery in heavy paediatric infection; "coconut cake" colonoscopy |
| **Ascaris lumbricoides** | Bile-stained, mamillated | **Ingested egg** | **Löffler pneumonitis** (larval lung migration), biliary/intestinal obstruction |
| **Hookworm (Ancylostoma, Necator)** | Segmented ovum | **FILARIFORM larva penetrates skin** | **Iron deficiency anaemia** |
| **Strongyloides stercoralis** | **RHABDITIFORM LARVA in stool** (not eggs) | Filariform larva, plus **autoinfection** | **Hyperinfection/disseminated disease with steroids or HTLV-1** |
| **Taenia saginata** | — | **Undercooked BEEF** | Adult tapeworm |
| **Taenia solium** | — | Undercooked **pork**; ingested **eggs → cysticercosis** | **Neurocysticercosis** |

- **Trichuris does NOT have a lung migration phase** — unlike Ascaris, hookworm and Strongyloides ("**heart–lung migration**").
- **Treatment: albendazole/mebendazole** for the soil-transmitted helminths (**Trichuris responds least well**; add ivermectin); **ivermectin** for Strongyloides; **praziquantel** for tapeworms and flukes.
- **India's National Deworming Day** — **albendazole 400 mg to all children 1–19 years, twice yearly**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Egg image → transmission route. **Plugged barrel = Trichuris =
swallowed egg**; the distractors offer larval penetration (hookworm/Strongyloides) or beef
(Taenia).

**REMEMBER:** *Plugged barrel egg goes in by mouth; larvae go in through the feet.*

`Q10939`

---

## 118. Sporicidal Agents

**PEARL:** The **sporicidal chemicals are EFGH — Ethylene oxide, Formaldehyde,
Glutaraldehyde, Hydrogen peroxide** — plus the **3 Ps: Peracetic acid, ortho-Phthalaldehyde
and Plasma sterilisation**. **Quaternary ammonium compounds (benzalkonium chloride) are
NOT sporicidal** — they are low-level disinfectants.

**UNDERSTAND:** A spore's resistance comes from its **dehydrated core, calcium
dipicolinate and thick keratin-like coat**, which excludes water-soluble agents. Only
chemicals that either **alkylate proteins and nucleic acids (ethylene oxide, formaldehyde,
glutaraldehyde)** or generate **oxidising free radicals (hydrogen peroxide, peracetic acid,
plasma)** can penetrate and kill it. Surface-active agents such as quaternary ammonium
compounds work by disrupting lipid membranes — a spore has none exposed, so they fail.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Spaulding classification:** **Critical items** (enter sterile tissue/vasculature — surgical instruments, implants) → **sterilisation**; **Semi-critical** (contact mucous membranes — endoscopes, laryngoscope blades) → **high-level disinfection** (2% glutaraldehyde, OPA, peracetic acid); **Non-critical** (intact skin — BP cuffs, stethoscopes) → low/intermediate-level disinfection.
- **Levels:** **high** — kills all including some spores (glutaraldehyde, hydrogen peroxide, peracetic acid, OPA); **intermediate** — kills mycobacteria, most viruses and fungi but not spores (**alcohols, chlorine/hypochlorite, iodophors, phenolics**); **low** — vegetative bacteria and lipid viruses only (**quaternary ammonium compounds**).
- **Chlorine is an intermediate-level disinfectant** — sporicidal only at very high concentrations/prolonged contact, so it is counted as **not sporicidal** in exam convention. It is, however, the agent of choice for **blood spills (1% hypochlorite)** and water disinfection.
- **Ethylene oxide** sterilises **heat-sensitive items** (plastics, endoscopes, pacemakers) but is toxic, mutagenic and needs long aeration; **plasma (hydrogen peroxide gas)** is the faster, safer modern alternative.
- **Alcohol is NOT sporicidal** — hence handwashing with **soap and water, not alcohol rub, for *Clostridioides difficile***.
- **Autoclave 121 °C at 15 psi for 15 minutes** is the standard sterilisation method; monitor with **spore strips of *Geobacillus stearothermophilus***; **hot air oven 160 °C for 2 hours** uses ***Bacillus atrophaeus*** spores.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Multi-select sporicidal lists. Learn **EFGH + 3P** and remember to
**exclude quaternary ammonium, alcohols and (by convention) chlorine**.

**REMEMBER:** *EFGH kills spores; soaps and alcohols do not.*

`Q10942`

---

## 119. Intrinsic Antifungal Resistance

**PEARL:** ***Candida krusei* is INTRINSICALLY RESISTANT TO FLUCONAZOLE.** *C. glabrata*
has reduced (dose-dependent) azole susceptibility; ***C. lusitaniae* is the one resistant
to amphotericin B**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Azoles work by inhibiting **lanosterol 14α-demethylase (ERG11)**, the enzyme
that makes ergosterol. In *C. krusei* the target enzyme has a **structurally reduced
affinity for fluconazole**, so the drug simply does not bind well — resistance is
**built into the species**, present before any drug exposure, and cannot be overcome by
raising the dose. Newer, bulkier azoles (**voriconazole, posaconazole**) bind adequately,
which is why they remain active.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Species and susceptibility:** *C. albicans* and *C. dubliniensis* — fully azole-susceptible; *C. glabrata* — dose-dependent, often needs an **echinocandin**; ***C. krusei* — fluconazole-resistant (use voriconazole or an echinocandin)**; ***C. lusitaniae* — amphotericin B resistant**; ***C. auris* — multidrug-resistant, a serious nosocomial outbreak organism** requiring strict contact precautions.
- **Echinocandins (caspofungin, micafungin, anidulafungin) — inhibit β-1,3-glucan synthase (cell wall)** — are **first-line for candidaemia and invasive candidiasis**; they have **poor urinary and CNS penetration** (use fluconazole or amphotericin for urinary and CNS disease).
- ***Aspergillus* is intrinsically resistant to fluconazole and to the echinocandins as monotherapy for meningitis; VORICONAZOLE is first-line for invasive aspergillosis** (isavuconazole an alternative).
- **Mucorales (Rhizopus, Mucor) are resistant to voriconazole** — treat **rhino-orbito-cerebral mucormycosis with liposomal amphotericin B + urgent surgical debridement + control of the underlying diabetic ketoacidosis**. Voriconazole prophylaxis is a recognised risk factor for breakthrough mucormycosis.
- ***Cryptococcus*: amphotericin B + flucytosine induction, then fluconazole**; ***Sporothrix*: itraconazole* is the drug of choice** (so that option pairing is wrong).
- Germ tube (Reynolds–Braude) test positive = *C. albicans*/*dubliniensis*; **CHROMagar** and MALDI-TOF speciate rapidly.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Match the organism to the drug it is intrinsically resistant to.
**krusei–fluconazole** and **lusitaniae–amphotericin** are the two pairs worth memorising.

**REMEMBER:** *Krusei says no to fluconazole from birth.*

`Q10970`

---

## 120. Erythema Migrans and the Named Erythemas

**PEARL:** An expanding **annular rash with central clearing ("bull's-eye") at least 5 cm
across**, appearing days to weeks after a tick bite in an endemic area, is **ERYTHEMA
MIGRANS — the pathognomonic rash of early Lyme disease (*Borrelia burgdorferi*)**. It is a
**clinical diagnosis; serology is often still negative and should not delay treatment.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The ring shape is the spirochaete's own migration: organisms spread
**centrifugally through the dermis** from the bite, and the immune response follows them
outward, so inflammation is greatest at the advancing edge while the centre clears. The
rash is therefore a map of the infection, not an allergic reaction — which is why it is
**painless, non-pruritic, warm, and expands over days**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Lyme stages:** **early localised** — erythema migrans, flu-like illness; **early disseminated (weeks–months)** — multiple secondary EM lesions, **facial (often bilateral) nerve palsy**, lymphocytic meningitis, radiculopathy, **AV block/Lyme carditis**; **late (months–years)** — **oligoarticular arthritis of the knee**, acrodermatitis chronica atrophicans, encephalopathy.
- **Vector: *Ixodes* tick**; transmission needs **≥36–48 hours of attachment**, so prompt removal prevents it. Same tick transmits **babesiosis and anaplasmosis**.
- **Treatment: doxycycline** (14–21 days) — **amoxicillin or cefuroxime** in children under 8 and pregnancy; **IV ceftriaxone** for neurological disease or high-grade heart block. **Jarisch–Herxheimer** reaction may occur.
- **Distinguish the named erythemas:**
  - **Erythema migrans** — Lyme, expanding, single or multiple, central clearing.
  - **Erythema marginatum** — **rheumatic fever** (a major Jones criterion): evanescent, non-pruritic, serpiginous pink rings on the **trunk**, sparing the face.
  - **Erythema multiforme** — **true target lesions with three zones**, **acral distribution (palms, soles, extensors)**, triggered by **HSV** (commonest) and *Mycoplasma*; mucosal involvement in the major form.
  - **Erythema annulare centrifugum** — slowly migrating annular plaques with a **trailing scale inside the advancing edge**.
  - **Erythema nodosum** — tender red **nodules on the shins** (streptococcus, sarcoidosis, TB, IBD, drugs, pregnancy).
  - **Erythema chronicum migrans is the older name for the Lyme rash.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The stem supplies the geography and the tick. Any **expanding
single ring after outdoor exposure** is erythema migrans; **targets on the palms** are
erythema multiforme.

**REMEMBER:** *One ring that grows = Lyme; many true targets on the hands = multiforme;
fleeting rings on the trunk with carditis = marginatum.*

`Q10945`

---

## 121. Vibrio cholerae Toxins and Tight Junctions

**PEARL:** *Vibrio cholerae* **HA/protease cleaves the extracellular domain of OCCLUDIN**,
and **zonula occludens toxin (ZOT)** loosens the tight junctions — both add a **paracellular
leak** to the massive transcellular secretion driven by cholera toxin.

**UNDERSTAND:** Cholera empties the gut by two complementary routes. **Cholera toxin (the
main one)** — an **AB₅ toxin whose A subunit ADP-ribosylates the Gs alpha subunit**, locking
adenylyl cyclase on → **persistently high cAMP → CFTR-mediated chloride and water secretion**.
That is the transcellular flood. **ZOT and the HA/protease** attack the **zonula occludens
(tight junction) proteins — occludin and the claudins** — opening the paracellular route as
well. Together they produce the **rice-water stool** of up to 20 litres a day.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Tight junction proteins: OCCLUDIN, CLAUDINS, JAMs, and the intracellular ZO-1/2/3.** They seal the **apical margins** of intestinal, renal tubular, choroid plexus and endothelial (**blood–brain barrier**) epithelium.
- **Cell junction map:** **zonula occludens (tight) — seals; zonula adherens — CADHERINS and catenins; macula adherens (desmosome) — DESMOGLEIN/desmocollin (target in pemphigus); hemidesmosome — INTEGRINS and BP180/BP230 (target in pemphigoid); gap junction — CONNEXINS**.
- ***V. cholerae*: comma-shaped, Gram-negative, oxidase POSITIVE, DARTING (shooting-star) motility on hanging drop, alkaline peptone water for enrichment, TCBS agar (yellow, sucrose-fermenting) for culture.** **O1 (classical and El Tor biotypes; Ogawa/Inaba/Hikojima serotypes) and O139** cause epidemics.
- **Treatment: ORAL REHYDRATION SOLUTION is the mainstay** — its success rests on the **sodium–glucose cotransporter (SGLT1), which is unaffected by cholera toxin**, so glucose drags sodium and water back in. Severe cases need **IV Ringer's lactate**. **Doxycycline (or azithromycin in children and pregnancy)** shortens the illness and the shedding.
- **Other AB₅/ADP-ribosylating toxins:** **pertussis toxin — ADP-ribosylates Gi (disinhibiting adenylyl cyclase)**; **diphtheria and Pseudomonas exotoxin A — ADP-ribosylate eEF-2**; **E. coli LT — identical to cholera toxin**; **shiga toxin — inactivates 60S ribosome**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The question names the **junction protein**. **Tight junction →
occludin/claudin**; adherens → cadherin; hemidesmosome → integrin. Cholera attacks the
**tight** junction.

**REMEMBER:** *Cholera opens both the door (CFTR) and the gaps between the doors (occludin).*

`Q11002`

---

## 122. Mucormycosis After COVID-19

**PEARL:** **Broad, ASEPTATE (pauci-septate), ribbon-like hyphae branching at WIDE (right)
ANGLES**, in a diabetic or steroid-treated post-COVID patient with **blood-tinged nasal
discharge and black necrotic turbinates**, is **MUCORMYCOSIS**. **Aspergillus** is the
opposite: **narrow, SEPTATE hyphae branching at ACUTE (45°) angles**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Mucorales are **angioinvasive** — they penetrate arterial walls, causing
**thrombosis, infarction and necrosis**, which is why the tissue turns black and why
antifungals alone fail: **the drug cannot reach dead, thrombosed tissue**. Hence the
absolute requirement for **surgical debridement alongside amphotericin B**. Their growth is
favoured by **acidosis and free iron** — ketoacidosis both lowers pH and releases iron from
transferrin, which is why **diabetic ketoacidosis is the classic setting**, compounded by
steroids and COVID-19-associated immune dysregulation.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Risk factors: uncontrolled diabetes (especially KETOACIDOSIS), corticosteroids, haematological malignancy, neutropenia, transplantation, iron overload and DEFEROXAMINE therapy (which acts as a siderophore for the fungus — use deferasirox instead), and COVID-19.**
- **Clinical forms:** **rhino-orbito-cerebral (commonest)** — facial pain and numbness, black nasal eschar, orbital apex syndrome, cavernous sinus thrombosis; also pulmonary, cutaneous (after trauma or contaminated dressings), gastrointestinal and disseminated.
- **Treatment: (1) reverse the predisposing condition — correct ketoacidosis, stop steroids; (2) URGENT and repeated SURGICAL DEBRIDEMENT; (3) LIPOSOMAL AMPHOTERICIN B** (high dose), with **posaconazole or isavuconazole** as step-down. **Voriconazole and echinocandins do NOT work — voriconazole prophylaxis is itself a risk factor for breakthrough mucormycosis.**
- Laboratory: **KOH mount and histopathology** are the fastest and most useful; culture on **Sabouraud agar at 25–37 °C gives fast-growing "lid-lifter" grey-white cottony colonies**; the fungus is often **not cultured** because grinding the specimen destroys the aseptate hyphae — **mince, do not grind**.
- **Aspergillosis** — allergic (**ABPA**: asthma, high IgE, central bronchiectasis), **aspergilloma** (fungal ball in a pre-existing cavity, **Monod sign**, haemoptysis), and **invasive** (**halo and air-crescent signs** on CT, galactomannan positive) — treat with **voriconazole**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The histopathology image is the whole question. **Broad, aseptate,
right-angle = Mucor; narrow, septate, acute-angle = Aspergillus.**

**REMEMBER:** *Ribbons at right angles, in a sugar-rich acidotic patient — cut it out and
give amphotericin.*

`Q10994`

---

## 123. Ticks vs Mites: Who Transmits What

**PEARL:** **HARD TICKS (Ixodidae — oval body, dorsal scutum, no antennae, 4 pairs of legs)
transmit: Ehrlichia and Anaplasma, BABESIOSIS, Lyme disease, CCHF, KYASANUR FOREST DISEASE,
tick typhus/Rocky Mountain spotted fever, tularaemia, Colorado tick fever, viral
encephalitis and TICK PARALYSIS.** **SCRUB TYPHUS (Orientia tsutsugamushi) is transmitted by
TROMBICULID MITES (chiggers), NOT by ticks.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Vector identity determines the epidemiology and therefore the exposure
history the exam gives you. **Ticks attach for days**, which is why transmission of Lyme
disease requires **36–48 hours of attachment** and why tick removal prevents disease. **Mite
larvae (chiggers) feed briefly on grass and scrub**, leaving a single **eschar** at the bite
site — the classic clue in scrub typhus.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Scrub typhus:** fever, headache, myalgia, regional **lymphadenopathy** and a painless **ESCHAR (black necrotic scab)** in a warm moist skin fold; complications include ARDS, myocarditis, meningoencephalitis and acute kidney injury. **Weil–Felix OX-K positive**; confirm with IgM ELISA/PCR. **Treatment: DOXYCYCLINE (azithromycin in pregnancy and children).** A leading cause of **acute undifferentiated febrile illness in India**.
- **Hard vs soft ticks: HARD ticks have a scutum and feed for days (Ixodes, Dermacentor, Rhipicephalus, HYALOMMA — the CCHF vector); SOFT ticks (Ornithodoros) have no scutum, feed briefly at night, and transmit ENDEMIC RELAPSING FEVER (Borrelia).**
- **KYASANUR FOREST DISEASE** — a flavivirus of the Western Ghats (Karnataka), transmitted by **Haemaphysalis ticks**, with monkeys as amplifying hosts; a **vaccine exists** for forest workers.
- **Tick paralysis** — an ascending flaccid paralysis from a neurotoxin in tick saliva, which **resolves dramatically on removing the tick** (remove with steady traction using forceps close to the skin; do not burn or twist).
- **Other arthropod vectors: LOUSE — epidemic typhus, relapsing fever, trench fever; FLEA — plague, murine typhus; SANDFLY — kala-azar, sandfly fever, Oroya fever; TSETSE FLY — African trypanosomiasis; REDUVIID BUG — Chagas disease; BLACKFLY (Simulium) — onchocerciasis; CYCLOPS — guinea worm and fish tapeworm.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** An image of the vector plus "which disease is NOT transmitted". Learn
the **tick list** and remember that **scrub typhus is the mite-borne outlier**.

**REMEMBER:** *Ticks carry almost everything — but the chigger keeps scrub typhus for
itself.*

`Q11023`

---

## 124. HIV Entry: gp120 and gp41

**PEARL:** **gp120 is the surface spike that BINDS CD4** and then the co-receptor
(**CCR5** for macrophage-tropic R5 strains, **CXCR4** for T-cell-tropic X4 strains); the
conformational change exposes **gp41**, the transmembrane protein that **fuses** the viral
envelope with the cell membrane.

**UNDERSTAND:** Entry is a **two-key lock**. CD4 binding alone is insufficient — it merely
repositions gp120 to expose the **V3 loop**, which then engages a chemokine co-receptor;
only that second engagement triggers gp41 to spring open and drive the fusion peptide into
the host membrane. This two-step requirement explains the natural resistance conferred by
the **CCR5-Δ32 homozygous deletion** (people who are effectively immune to R5 HIV) and the
existence of two separate drug classes — **maraviroc (a CCR5 antagonist)** and
**enfuvirtide (a gp41 fusion inhibitor)**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **env gene → gp160 → cleaved into gp120 (surface) and gp41 (transmembrane).** **gag → p24 core antigen (the antigen detected in 4th-generation ELISA)**; **pol → reverse transcriptase, integrase and protease**.
- **R5 strains predominate in early infection (transmitted strains); a switch to X4 tropism heralds rapid CD4 decline** and disease progression. **Test tropism before prescribing maraviroc.**
- **gp120 is hypervariable** — this and the glycan shield are the principal reasons **no effective vaccine exists**.
- **Target cells: CD4+ helper T cells (worst affected), macrophages/monocytes (a long-lived reservoir), dendritic and microglial cells.** Dendritic cells carry virus to lymph nodes via **DC-SIGN**.
- **Diagnosis: 4th-generation combined antigen/antibody assay (detects p24 from ~2–3 weeks, shortening the window period); confirm and monitor with HIV RNA viral load; CD4 count stages the immunodeficiency.**
- **First-line ART (WHO/India): TENOFOVIR + LAMIVUDINE + DOLUTEGRAVIR (TLD).**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The question separates **attachment (gp120)** from **fusion/penetration
(gp41)** and from the **host co-receptors (CCR5/CXCR4)**. Read the verb in the stem.

**REMEMBER:** *gp120 grabs, gp41 fuses.*

`Q11036`

---

## 125. Biosafety Levels

**PEARL:** **BSL-3 = agents causing serious or lethal disease that are transmissible by
INHALATION, but for which treatment or vaccines exist** — **Mycobacterium tuberculosis
(including MDR/XDR), anthrax, brucella, coxiella, hantavirus, SARS-CoV-2, West Nile,
yellow fever, Rickettsia, HIV cultures**. **BSL-4 is for agents with no treatment or
vaccine and high aerosol lethality — EBOLA, Marburg, Lassa, CCHF, smallpox.**
***Salmonella typhi* is BSL-2.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The classification is decided by **route of transmission plus the
availability of treatment**, not simply by how deadly the organism is. That is why *Salmonella
typhi*, which can kill, is only BSL-2 — it spreads by ingestion and is treatable, so
standard bench precautions suffice. Tuberculosis, by contrast, is **airborne**, so it needs
**negative-pressure containment and directional airflow** even though it is curable.

**MUST KNOW:**

| Level | Agents | Requirements |
|---|---|---|
| **BSL-1** | Non-pathogenic (*E. coli* K12, *B. subtilis*) | Standard bench, open work |
| **BSL-2** | Moderate risk, ingestion/mucosal exposure — **Salmonella, Staphylococcus, hepatitis B, HIV (clinical specimens), influenza** | **Biosafety cabinet for aerosol-generating procedures, limited access, autoclave** |
| **BSL-3** | **Serious/lethal, AEROSOL transmission, treatment available** — **M. tuberculosis, Brucella, Coxiella, anthrax, SARS-CoV-2, yellow fever, HIV cultures** | **Negative pressure, directional airflow, all work in a biosafety cabinet, respirators, controlled access** |
| **BSL-4** | **Lethal, aerosol, NO treatment/vaccine** — **Ebola, Marburg, Lassa, CCHF, smallpox, Nipah** | **Full positive-pressure suit or class III cabinet, dedicated building, airlocks, shower-out** |

- **Brucella and Coxiella are among the commonest laboratory-acquired infections** — always warn the laboratory when you suspect them.
- **Coccidioides** arthroconidia are also handled at BSL-3.
- **India has BSL-4 facilities at NIV Pune** (used for Nipah and CCHF).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Sort by **route + treatability**. TB is the archetypal BSL-3 answer;
haemorrhagic fever viruses are BSL-4; enteric bacteria are BSL-2.

**REMEMBER:** *Airborne but curable is level 3; airborne and untreatable is level 4.*

`Q11033`

---

## 126. Superantigens

**PEARL:** A toxin that **cross-links the Vβ region of the T-cell receptor directly to MHC
class II OUTSIDE the peptide groove** — activating up to 20% of all T cells regardless of
their antigen specificity — is a **SUPERANTIGEN**. Examples: **streptococcal pyrogenic
exotoxin (SPE), staphylococcal TSST-1 and enterotoxins**.

**UNDERSTAND:** A conventional antigen must be **processed, loaded into the MHC groove, and
recognised by a matching TCR** — which is why only about 1 in 10,000 T cells respond. A
superantigen **bypasses processing entirely**, clamping the outside of the MHC II molecule
to the outside of the TCR β chain. Because the binding does not depend on specificity, an
enormous clone of T cells fires at once, releasing **IFN-γ, which drives macrophages to
release IL-1, IL-6 and TNF-α** — the **cytokine storm** that produces shock and multi-organ
failure within hours.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Toxic shock syndrome: fever, hypotension, a diffuse macular ERYTHRODERMA with later DESQUAMATION of the palms and soles, and multi-organ involvement.** **Staphylococcal TSS** — classically tampon-associated or from a surgical/nasal packing site, often with **no positive blood culture**. **Streptococcal TSS** — usually accompanies **necrotising fasciitis**, and blood cultures are **usually positive**.
- **Treatment: fluid resuscitation, source removal/surgical debridement, and CLINDAMYCIN ADDED to a beta-lactam** — clindamycin is a protein-synthesis inhibitor, so it **switches off toxin production** ("Eagle effect"); IVIG neutralises circulating toxin.
- Contrast the other classic toxin mechanisms:
  - **ADP-ribosylation of EF-2 → DIPHTHERIA toxin and Pseudomonas exotoxin A** (inhibit protein synthesis).
  - **ADP-ribosylation of Gs → cholera toxin and E. coli LT** (↑cAMP, secretory diarrhoea); **of Gi → pertussis toxin**.
  - **Cleavage of SNARE proteins → BOTULINUM (blocks ACh release at the NMJ → flaccid, descending paralysis) and TETANUS (blocks glycine/GABA release from Renshaw cells → spastic paralysis)**.
  - **Ribosome inactivation (28S rRNA) → shiga and shiga-like toxin**.
  - **Glucosylation of Rho GTPases → Clostridioides difficile toxins A and B** (cytoskeletal disruption, pseudomembranous colitis).
- Superantigens are also produced by **Mycoplasma arthritidis and Yersinia**; some viral proteins act similarly.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The diagram showing a toxin bridging MHC II and the TCR **from the
outside** is unique to superantigens. Everything binding a ribosome or a G protein is a
different mechanism.

**REMEMBER:** *A superantigen skips the groove and shouts at every T cell at once.*

`Q11051`

---

## 127. Diphtheria Toxin

**PEARL:** **Diphtheria toxin is an A–B exotoxin whose A subunit ADP-RIBOSYLATES ELONGATION
FACTOR 2 (EF-2)**, halting protein synthesis and killing the cell. **Pseudomonas exotoxin A
acts identically.**

**UNDERSTAND:** EF-2 is required for **translocation of the ribosome along the mRNA** at
every cycle of elongation. Modifying it stops **all** protein synthesis in the cell —
which is why the toxin is lethal at extraordinarily low doses (a single molecule can kill
a cell) and why the damage is **not confined to the throat**: circulating toxin reaches the
heart, kidneys, nerves and adrenals.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The toxin is encoded by the tox gene carried on a LYSOGENIC BETA-PHAGE** — only lysogenised strains are toxigenic (the same principle as botulinum, shiga, cholera and streptococcal erythrogenic toxins).
- Clinical: sore throat with a **greyish adherent PSEUDOMEMBRANE that BLEEDS on removal**, **"BULL NECK"** cervical lymphadenitis, and airway obstruction. Toxic complications — **MYOCARDITIS (the commonest cause of death, at 1–2 weeks)** and **palatal and ciliary PARALYSIS/polyneuritis (3–7 weeks)**, plus tubular necrosis.
- **Treatment: ANTITOXIN FIRST — it neutralises only circulating (unbound) toxin, so it must not be delayed for laboratory confirmation** — plus penicillin or erythromycin to stop toxin production and transmission, isolation, and airway care.
- **Diagnosis: Albert's/Neisser's stain shows METACHROMATIC (volutin) GRANULES and Chinese-letter arrangement**; culture on **Loeffler's serum slope** (enrichment) and **potassium tellurite/McLeod's medium (black colonies)**; toxigenicity by the **ELEK gel precipitation test** or PCR for tox.
- **Prevention is by TOXOID (DPT/Td)**, which produces antitoxic — not antibacterial — immunity; the **Schick test** (historical) measured susceptibility.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** A mechanism diagram showing **EF-2 being inactivated**. Diphtheria and
Pseudomonas exotoxin A are the two answers.

**REMEMBER:** *Diphtheria stops the ribosome from stepping forward — EF-2 is the target.*

`Q11045`

---

## 128. Indirect Immunofluorescence

**PEARL:** **INDIRECT immunofluorescence is the standard method for detecting circulating
AUTOANTIBODIES** (ANA, ANCA, anti-dsDNA) — patient serum is layered on a known antigen
substrate, then a **fluorescein-labelled ANTI-HUMAN IMMUNOGLOBULIN** is added to reveal any
bound antibody.

**UNDERSTAND:** The "indirect" step is what makes it work for **any** autoantibody. Because
the fluorescent label is on a **generic anti-human IgG**, one reagent detects antibodies of
every specificity — you only change the substrate (HEp-2 cells for ANA, neutrophils for
ANCA, *Crithidia* for anti-dsDNA). It also **amplifies the signal** (several labelled
antibodies bind each patient antibody), making it more sensitive than the direct method.
**DIRECT immunofluorescence**, by contrast, applies a labelled antibody straight onto a
**tissue biopsy** to find **immunoglobulin already deposited there** — which is why it is
the test for **pemphigus, pemphigoid and glomerulonephritis**, not for serum.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **ANA patterns on HEp-2 cells: HOMOGENEOUS → SLE and drug-induced lupus (anti-dsDNA, anti-histone); SPECKLED → mixed connective tissue disease/Sjögren (anti-Sm, RNP, Ro/La); NUCLEOLAR → SCLERODERMA (anti-Scl-70); CENTROMERE → LIMITED cutaneous scleroderma/CREST; RIM/peripheral → anti-dsDNA, most specific for SLE.**
- **ANCA: c-ANCA (cytoplasmic, anti-PR3) → granulomatosis with polyangiitis; p-ANCA (perinuclear, anti-MPO) → microscopic polyangiitis and eosinophilic granulomatosis with polyangiitis.**
- **Direct IF findings: LINEAR IgG along the basement membrane → BULLOUS PEMPHIGOID and anti-GBM (Goodpasture) disease; INTERCELLULAR "fishnet" IgG → PEMPHIGUS VULGARIS; GRANULAR IgA in dermal papillae → DERMATITIS HERPETIFORMIS; "full house" granular deposits → lupus nephritis.**
- Other techniques and their uses: **ELISA — quantifies a specific antigen or antibody (HIV, hepatitis serology, hormone assays); FLOW CYTOMETRY — counts and phenotypes CELLS (CD4 count, leukaemia immunophenotyping); FISH — locates DNA SEQUENCES on chromosomes (HER2 amplification, 22q11 deletion); Western blot — protein identification; PCR — nucleic acid amplification.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Read whether the target is in **serum (indirect IF, ELISA)** or in
**tissue (direct IF)**, and whether it is a **cell (flow cytometry)** or a **gene (FISH)**.

**REMEMBER:** *Indirect finds antibodies floating in serum; direct finds them already stuck
in tissue.*

`Q11047`

---

## 129. Molecular Basis of Antitubercular Drug Resistance

**PEARL:** **Rifampicin → rpoB (RNA polymerase β-subunit). Isoniazid → katG (failure of
PRODRUG ACTIVATION) and inhA. Pyrazinamide → pncA (pyrazinamidase).
Ethambutol → embB (arabinosyl transferase). FLUOROQUINOLONES → gyrA/gyrB (DNA gyrase)** —
so "DNA gyrase mutation causing pyrazinamide resistance" is false.

**UNDERSTAND:** Resistance always arises at **the drug's own molecular target or at the
enzyme that activates it**. Isoniazid is unique in being a **PRODRUG requiring the
mycobacterial catalase-peroxidase KatG** to convert it into the active radical that inhibits
InhA (mycolic acid synthesis) — so losing katG confers resistance **without any change to the
target**. Pyrazinamide is likewise a **prodrug activated by pyrazinamidase (pncA)** to
pyrazinoic acid, active only in the **acidic environment of macrophage phagolysosomes**,
which is why it is uniquely good at killing intracellular semi-dormant bacilli in the first
two months.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **rpoB mutations are the basis of the rapid molecular tests: CBNAAT/Xpert MTB/RIF and Truenat detect rifampicin resistance in ~2 hours; LINE PROBE ASSAY (LPA) detects rifampicin and isoniazid (and second-line) resistance.** **Rifampicin resistance is the sentinel marker for MDR-TB** because it rarely occurs alone.
- **Pyrazinamidase activity distinguishes species: *M. tuberculosis* is pyrazinamidase POSITIVE; *M. bovis* is NEGATIVE and therefore intrinsically pyrazinamide-resistant.**
- **First-line drug mechanisms: isoniazid and ethionamide — mycolic acid synthesis (InhA); rifampicin — DNA-dependent RNA polymerase; ethambutol — arabinosyl transferase (arabinogalactan of the cell wall); pyrazinamide — membrane transport/fatty acid synthase in acid pH; streptomycin — 30S ribosome (rpsL/rrs).**
- **Resistance in TB is CHROMOSOMAL and arises by spontaneous mutation under selection — it is not plasmid-mediated**, which is the reason multidrug regimens work (the probability of simultaneous mutations to two drugs is vanishingly small) and why **monotherapy or erratic adherence creates resistance**.
- **Bedaquiline (ATP synthase), linezolid (50S), delamanid/pretomanid (mycolic acid synthesis and nitric oxide release)** are the Group A/C agents whose resistance now defines pre-XDR and XDR TB.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Match drug to gene. The recurring trap swaps **gyrase (quinolones)**
onto pyrazinamide.

**REMEMBER:** *INH and pyrazinamide are prodrugs — kill the activator and you kill the drug.*

`Q11795`

---

## 130. Non-Tuberculous Mycobacteria: Fish Tank Granuloma

**PEARL:** A granulomatous nodular or verrucous skin lesion on the hand of someone who
**handles fish or aquariums**, weeks after a minor injury, is **fish-tank (swimming-pool)
granuloma — *Mycobacterium marinum***, a **PHOTOCHROMOGEN (Runyon group I): NO pigment in
the dark, YELLOW pigment after light exposure.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Photochromogenicity is a genuine physiological trait — light **induces
transcription of carotenoid biosynthesis genes**, so the colony turns yellow only after
exposure. *M. marinum* also has a **low optimal growth temperature (30–32 °C)**, which is why
it grows in cool water and infects only the **cooler peripheral skin — hands, forearms,
knees** — and never disseminates in the immunocompetent.

**MUST KNOW — RUNYON classification of non-tuberculous mycobacteria:**
- **Group I — PHOTOCHROMOGENS (pigment only after light): *M. marinum* (fish tank granuloma), *M. kansasii* (TB-like pulmonary disease).**
- **Group II — SCOTOCHROMOGENS (pigment in the DARK): *M. scrofulaceum* (cervical lymphadenitis in children), *M. gordonae*.**
- **Group III — NON-chromogens: *M. avium-intracellulare* complex (MAC — disseminated disease in AIDS with CD4 <50; treat/prophylax with a macrolide), *M. ulcerans* (BURULI ulcer — a large painless undermined ulcer from mycolactone toxin), *M. xenopi*.**
- **Group IV — RAPID GROWERS (visible growth in <7 days): *M. fortuitum*, *M. chelonae*, *M. abscessus*** — classic causes of **post-injection abscesses, surgical site and catheter infections, and post-laparoscopic wound infections** (contaminated instrument disinfection).
- ***M. marinum*: lesions may ascend along lymphatics in a SPOROTRICHOID pattern; treatment is prolonged (clarithromycin, doxycycline, rifampicin + ethambutol) ± surgical excision. Cultures must be incubated at 30 °C** — the laboratory will miss it at 37 °C, so tell them the exposure history.
- **NTM are environmental (water and soil), NOT transmitted person to person**, and are typically **resistant to standard antitubercular regimens**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The exposure history is the diagnosis; the question then tests the
**Runyon group**. Aquarium/fish → *M. marinum* → **photochromogen**.

**REMEMBER:** *Aquarium hand, cool-loving mycobacterium that only colours in the light.*

`Q11813`

---

## 131. Pneumocystis jirovecii

**PEARL:** *Pneumocystis jirovecii* **CANNOT be cultured** — not on Sabouraud agar or any
routine medium. Diagnosis is **morphological**: the **CYST WALL stains BLACK with
methenamine SILVER** (also toluidine blue, calcofluor white), while **trophozoites (thin-
walled, irregular) are seen on GIEMSA**; **immunofluorescence and PCR** on induced sputum or
bronchoalveolar lavage are the modern tests.

**UNDERSTAND:** It is a **fungus by genome (rRNA sequencing) but behaves like a protozoan**:
its membrane contains **cholesterol rather than ergosterol**, which is exactly why
**amphotericin B and azoles do not work** and why treatment is with **co-trimoxazole**
(targeting folate synthesis). Its inability to be cultured reflects an obligate dependence on
the alveolar surface for growth — hence diagnosis rests on **seeing the organism**, not
growing it.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Clinical: HIV with CD4 <200 (or steroids, transplantation, malignancy) presenting with a DRY cough, progressive dyspnoea, fever and marked EXERTIONAL DESATURATION with a relatively normal chest examination.** **Chest X-ray: bilateral perihilar/"bat-wing" interstitial infiltrates** (may be normal early); **HRCT shows GROUND-GLASS opacities**; **raised LDH and (1,3)-β-D-glucan** support the diagnosis. **Pneumothorax** is a classic complication.
- **Treatment: high-dose CO-TRIMOXAZOLE (TMP-SMX) for 21 days.** **ADD CORTICOSTEROIDS if PaO₂ <70 mmHg or the A–a gradient >35 mmHg** — steroids reduce mortality by blunting the inflammatory surge as organisms die. Alternatives: **pentamidine, clindamycin–primaquine, atovaquone, dapsone–trimethoprim** (check **G6PD** before dapsone/primaquine).
- **PROPHYLAXIS with co-trimoxazole when CD4 <200**, after an episode, or in prolonged immunosuppression; stop when CD4 recovers above 200 for 3 months on ART.
- **Start ART within 2 weeks** of beginning PCP treatment (unlike cryptococcal meningitis, where ART is delayed).
- **Extrapulmonary Pneumocystis** is rare and associated with aerosolised pentamidine prophylaxis.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Which statement is FALSE?" — the false one is always about **culture**.
Everything about stains, morphology and co-trimoxazole is true.

**REMEMBER:** *You cannot grow it — you can only stain it black with silver.*

`Q11819`

---

## 132. Severe Dengue — WHO Criteria

**PEARL:** **AST or ALT ≥ 1000 IU/L** is a criterion for **SEVERE dengue**. Persistent vomiting, hepatomegaly > 2 cm and rising haematocrit with falling platelets are **WARNING SIGNS**, not severe-disease criteria.

**UNDERSTAND:** The 2009 WHO revision moved away from the old DHF/DSS grading to a **severity-based** scheme built on **organ failure**. Transaminases in the thousands signify **fulminant hepatitis**, an actual organ failure — whereas vomiting and hepatomegaly merely predict that the patient is entering the **critical (plasma-leak) phase** and needs observation.

**MUST KNOW:**
