- **Severe dengue = any one of:** (1) **severe plasma leakage** → shock (DSS), fluid accumulation with respiratory distress; (2) **severe bleeding**; (3) **severe organ involvement** — **AST/ALT ≥ 1000**, impaired consciousness/encephalitis, **myocarditis**, renal failure.
- **Warning signs:** abdominal pain/tenderness, **persistent vomiting**, clinical fluid accumulation, mucosal bleeding, lethargy/restlessness, **liver enlargement > 2 cm**, **rising haematocrit with rapid fall in platelet count**.
- Three phases: **febrile → critical (defervescence, days 3–7, when leak occurs) → recovery**. Paradoxically, the danger begins **as the fever settles**.
- Diagnostics by timing: **NS1 antigen and RT-PCR in days 1–5**; **IgM from day 5**; **tourniquet test** supports capillary fragility.
- Management: **crystalloids**, no NSAIDs or aspirin (use paracetamol), platelet transfusion **only for significant bleeding** — not for a number alone.
- Four serotypes **DENV 1–4**, *Aedes aegypti*, **Flavivirus**. **Secondary infection with a different serotype** raises severe-disease risk (antibody-dependent enhancement).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The stem lists three warning signs and one severity criterion. Learn which side of the line each item sits on.

**REMEMBER:** *Warning signs predict; organ failure defines.*

`MG1192`

---

## 133. Egg Inoculation Sites for Virus Culture

**PEARL:** **Influenza vaccine virus is grown in the ALLANTOIC CAVITY** of the embryonated hen's egg.

**UNDERSTAND:** Each site suits a different virus because of the **tissue it exposes**. The allantoic cavity offers a **large fluid volume with high viral yield** — ideal for the bulk production a vaccine needs. The amniotic sac gives lower yield but supports **primary isolation** of influenza from clinical samples, which is why the two are used for different purposes.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Chorioallantoic membrane (CAM):** produces visible **pocks** — **herpes simplex, variola/vaccinia** (pock morphology once distinguished them).
- **Allantoic cavity:** **influenza vaccine production**, also mumps, Newcastle disease, duck-embryo rabies vaccine, and **yellow fever 17D vaccine**.
- **Amniotic sac:** **primary isolation of influenza** from clinical specimens; mumps.
- **Yolk sac:** **Chlamydia, Rickettsia, and some viruses**.
- Because vaccine is egg-grown, **egg allergy** was a traditional caution — now largely superseded by cell-culture and recombinant vaccines.
- Viruses are **obligate intracellular parasites**, hence live systems (eggs, cell culture, animals) are required; embryonated eggs were first used in **1931**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Labelled diagram of the embryonated egg. The trap is picking the **amniotic sac** — correct for isolation, wrong for vaccine manufacture.

**REMEMBER:** *Allantoic for vaccine, amniotic for isolation.*

`MA7575`

---

## 134. Bacterial Generation Times

**PEARL:** **M. leprae: 12–14 DAYS** (the slowest known bacterial pathogen). **M. tuberculosis: 15–20 hours. Coliforms (E. coli): 20 minutes.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Generation time drives **everything clinical**: incubation period, drug duration and culture time. Leprosy's fortnight-long doubling explains why the incubation period is **2–5 years**, why treatment lasts **6–12 months of multidrug therapy**, and why *M. leprae* **cannot be cultured in artificial media** at all — it is grown in the **mouse footpad** or the **nine-banded armadillo**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Slow growth also explains why **TB treatment lasts 6 months** and why **solid culture (Löwenstein–Jensen) takes 6–8 weeks**, while liquid (MGIT) takes 1–3 weeks.
- Mycobacterial cell wall **mycolic acid** confers **acid-fastness**; decolouriser strength differentiates species: **M. tuberculosis resists 20–25% sulphuric acid**; **M. leprae and coccidian oocysts (Cryptosporidium, Cyclospora, Cystoisospora) only 5%**; **Nocardia only 1%** — hence the **modified (Kinyoun) acid-fast stain** is required to demonstrate Nocardia.
- **Nocardia**: **Gram-positive, weakly acid-fast, branching filamentous rods**, catalase and urease positive, **aerobic**. Contrast **Actinomyces** — **anaerobic, NOT acid-fast**, sulphur granules.
- **Albert's stain** (for *Corynebacterium diphtheriae* metachromatic/volutin/Babes–Ernst granules) contains **toluidine blue O and malachite green** — green bacilli with **bluish-purple granules** in **cuneiform (Chinese-letter) arrangement**. Neisser's and Ponder's stains do the same job.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Pure number recall — and note the units, since "12–13 days" and "12–13 hours" are both offered.

**REMEMBER:** *Leprosy doubles in a fortnight; E. coli in a coffee break.*

`MA2064, MF7471, MD0949, MC7102`

---

## 135. Interferons

**PEARL:** **IFN-α (leucocytes) and IFN-β (fibroblasts and epithelium) are the ANTIVIRAL (type I) interferons.** **IFN-γ (T cells and NK cells) is the IMMUNE (type II) interferon** — a macrophage activator.

**UNDERSTAND:** Type I interferons are made by **virtually any infected cell** as an emergency alarm — they induce an **antiviral state in neighbouring cells** by switching on RNase L and protein kinase R. Type II is made only by **activated lymphocytes**, so it arrives later and its job is **directing the immune response**, not blocking viral replication directly.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Interferons are **species-specific but NOT virus-specific**, non-antigenic, protein (so **inactivated by proteases**), and stable at pH 2–10 — **except IFN-γ, which is acid-labile**.
- **IFN-γ activates macrophages** (essential for killing intracellular organisms — its receptor defect causes disseminated **BCG and atypical mycobacterial** infection), **upregulates MHC class I and II**, and drives **Th1** responses.
- **IFN-α** is used therapeutically in **chronic hepatitis B and C, hairy cell leukaemia, CML, Kaposi sarcoma, melanoma**; **IFN-β** in **multiple sclerosis**; **IFN-γ** in **chronic granulomatous disease** and osteopetrosis.
- Type I interferon also **enhances NK cell activity and MHC class I expression**, marking infected cells for CD8 killing.
- Key cytokines to keep separate: **IL-1, IL-6, TNF-α — fever/acute phase**; **IL-2 — T cell growth**; **IL-4 — Th2, IgE switching**; **IL-5 — eosinophils, IgA**; **IL-10 and TGF-β — anti-inflammatory**; **IL-12 — Th1 differentiation**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "High IFN-β → what infection?" → **viral**. Distinguish the source cell for each interferon; that is what the options usually test.

**REMEMBER:** *Alpha and beta fight the virus; gamma commands the immune system.*

`MC7052`

---

## 136. Meningococcus vs Gonococcus

**PEARL:** **Meningococcus ferments glucose AND maltose; gonococcus ferments glucose ONLY.** Maltose is the discriminator.

**UNDERSTAND:** Both are **oxidase-positive Gram-negative diplococci** with identical morphology on Gram stain, so identification must be **biochemical**. Neither reduces nitrate (only *N. mucosa* does). The maltose reaction is therefore the single reliable, cheap differentiator — the basis of the classic **"MG-G"** rule.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Meningococcus:** **capsulated**, **lens/half-moon** shaped, found **intra- AND extracellularly**, spread by **respiratory droplets**, **vaccine available** (polysaccharide/conjugate for A, C, W, Y; **protein-based for group B** since its capsule mimics human neural NCAM).
- **Gonococcus:** **non-capsulated**, **kidney-bean** shaped, **predominantly intracellular within neutrophils**, **sexually/perinatally transmitted**, **no vaccine** (pilin antigenic variation), carries **plasmids coding drug resistance**.
- Gonorrhoea treatment (CDC 2021): **ceftriaxone 500 mg IM single dose**; oral **cefixime 800 mg stat** where injectable is unavailable. Treat presumptively for **chlamydia with doxycycline** unless excluded.
- Gram stain of urethral discharge showing **intracellular Gram-negative diplococci** is diagnostic in **symptomatic men** (sensitivity > 95%), but not in women or at extragenital sites — use **NAAT**.
- **Neisseria have LOS (lipo-oligosaccharide), not LPS** — no repeating O antigen.
- **Terminal complement (C5–C9) deficiency → recurrent Neisseria infection.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Both organisms are oxidase-positive and ferment glucose — options A and B are deliberately shared. Only **maltose** separates them.

**REMEMBER:** *Meningococcus Gets Maltose; Gonococcus Gets Glucose only.*

`MA1622, MG7084, MD6628, MD6640`

---

## 137. Food Poisoning by Incubation Period

**PEARL:** Symptoms within **1–6 hours = preformed toxin — Staphylococcus aureus** (or **Bacillus cereus** emetic type). A **food handler carrier** points to **S. aureus**, since *B. cereus* has no human carrier state.

**UNDERSTAND:** The incubation period tells you **whether the toxin was already in the food**. A **preformed** toxin needs only absorption — hence symptoms in a few hours, dominated by **vomiting**. If the organism must **multiply and then produce toxin in the gut**, that takes 8–16 hours; if it must **invade the mucosa**, longer still, and the illness becomes **inflammatory diarrhoea with fever**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **1–6 h (preformed toxin, vomiting predominant):** **S. aureus** (ham, poultry, custard, mayonnaise, cream pastries — the **heat-stable enterotoxin survives cooking**); **B. cereus emetic type** (**reheated fried rice**).
- **8–16 h (toxin formed in vivo, cramps and diarrhoea):** **Clostridium perfringens** (meat, gravies), **B. cereus diarrhoeal type**.
- **> 16 h:** **Vibrio cholerae** (watery, shellfish), **Salmonella** (inflammatory, eggs/poultry), **ETEC** (watery, traveller's), **EHEC** (bloody, undercooked beef, **HUS risk — avoid antibiotics**), **Campylobacter** (inflammatory, poultry, **Guillain–Barré** sequel), **Listeria** (soft cheese, pregnancy).
- *S. aureus* enterotoxins are **superantigens** — they act partly by massive cytokine release.
- **Botulism** — preformed neurotoxin in home-canned foods — presents at **12–36 h** with **descending flaccid paralysis**, not gastroenteritis.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Read the **time interval** and the **food**. A cook/carrier in the stem is the extra clue selecting *S. aureus* over *B. cereus*.

**REMEMBER:** *Fast and vomiting means the toxin came ready-made.*

`MC5087`

---

## 138. Encapsulated Organisms and Post-Splenectomy Infection

**PEARL:** Splenectomy predisposes to **encapsulated organisms** — **Streptococcus pneumoniae (commonest), Haemophilus influenzae, Neisseria meningitidis, Klebsiella, Salmonella**. **Staphylococcus aureus is LEAST likely** — it is cleared by **neutrophils**, not by splenic opsonophagocytosis.

**UNDERSTAND:** The spleen's unique role is **removing opsonised, poorly-phagocytosed encapsulated bacteria from the bloodstream** and producing **IgM against polysaccharide antigens**. Capsules resist complement and neutrophil uptake, so those organisms *depend* on splenic clearance. Organisms handled well by neutrophils in tissue — like *S. aureus* — do not become a special hazard.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **OPSI (overwhelming post-splenectomy infection)** — fulminant sepsis, highest risk in the **first 2–3 years** and in children; lifelong residual risk.
- Prevention: **vaccinate ≥ 2 weeks BEFORE elective splenectomy** (or 2 weeks after emergency splenectomy) with **pneumococcal, meningococcal and Hib**; annual **influenza**; consider **prophylactic penicillin** and give a **standby antibiotic supply** with a medical alert card.
- **Capsule chemistry:** **polysaccharide** in most (pneumococcus, Klebsiella, Hib, meningococcus, *Salmonella* Typhi Vi, **Cryptococcus**, Bordetella); **hyaluronic acid** in **Strep. pyogenes** (non-immunogenic — it is a host molecule); **poly-D-glutamate (polypeptide)** in **Bacillus anthracis**; **F1 protein** in **Yersinia pestis**.
- Among clostridia, **C. perfringens is capsulated and NON-motile** — the double exception (all other clostridia are motile and non-capsulated; *C. tetani* type VI is the other non-motile one).
- Blood film after splenectomy: **Howell–Jolly bodies, target cells, thrombocytosis**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Least likely" → **S. aureus**. It does have a capsule, which is the trap — the reasoning must be about *clearance mechanism*, not capsule presence.

**REMEMBER:** *The spleen handles capsules; neutrophils handle staph.*

`MA1593, MC6978`

---

## 139. Draughtsman Colonies and Pneumococcal Autolysin

**PEARL:** The **draughtsman (carrom-coin) colony** of *Streptococcus pneumoniae* — central umbonation with concentric rings — is produced by **AUTOLYSIN**, which also causes **bile solubility**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Autolysin is an **amidase that cleaves peptidoglycan**. Older colonies at the centre lyse first, collapsing the middle and leaving the raised rim — hence the draughtsman appearance develops only **on prolonged incubation**. **Bile salts activate the same enzyme**, which is why pneumococci dissolve in bile while viridans streptococci do not.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Pneumococcal identification: **α-haemolytic**, **optochin-sensitive**, **bile-soluble**, **capsulated (Quellung/capsule swelling reaction)**, **lancet-shaped Gram-positive diplococci**. Viridans streptococci are optochin-resistant and bile-insoluble.
- **Autolysin also releases pneumolysin and cell wall fragments**, amplifying inflammation — which is why **antibiotic-induced lysis worsens inflammation in meningitis** and why **dexamethasone is given before or with the first antibiotic dose**.
- **Pneumolysin** is a cholesterol-dependent cytolysin causing the α-haemolysis (green) and tissue injury.
- **Capsule is the principal virulence factor**; > 90 serotypes; vaccines are **PCV13/PCV15/PCV20 conjugate** and **PPSV23 polysaccharide**.
- Commonest cause of **community-acquired pneumonia, adult bacterial meningitis, otitis media and sinusitis**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Straight cause-and-effect — the colony morphology maps to **autolysin**, not to the capsule or pneumolysin.

**REMEMBER:** *The enzyme that eats the colony's centre also dissolves it in bile.*

`MA1591`

---

## 140. Hereditary Angioedema and Complement Deficiencies

**PEARL:** **Recurrent, NON-ITCHY, non-urticarial angioedema with a family history = C1 INHIBITOR deficiency** — the swelling is **bradykinin**-mediated, not histamine-mediated.

**UNDERSTAND:** C1-INH normally restrains **C1, kallikrein and factor XIIa**. Without it, **kallikrein runs unchecked and generates bradykinin**, a potent vasodilator and permeability factor. Because **no mast cells and no histamine** are involved, there is **no itch, no urticaria, and no response to antihistamines, steroids or adrenaline** — the single most important clinical consequence.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Autosomal dominant**; attacks affect **face, limbs, larynx (life-threatening) and gut (recurrent abdominal pain mimicking an acute abdomen)**; triggered by **trauma, dental work, stress, ACE inhibitors** (which block bradykinin breakdown — **absolutely contraindicated**).
- Labs: **low C4 (best screening test, low even between attacks)**, low C1-INH level or function; **C1q is low in the ACQUIRED form** (associated with lymphoproliferative disease) and normal in hereditary.
- Acute treatment: **C1-INH concentrate**, **icatibant (bradykinin B2 antagonist)**, **ecallantide**; **fresh frozen plasma** if nothing else is available. Prophylaxis: **danazol/attenuated androgens**, **lanadelumab**, tranexamic acid.
- **Complement deficiency map:** **C1, C2, C4 → immune complex disease (SLE-like)**; **C3 and factor I → pyogenic infection with capsulated organisms**; **C5–C9 (MAC) → recurrent Neisseria**; **factor D/properdin → Neisseria and capsulated organisms**; **DAF/CD59 (GPI anchor) → paroxysmal nocturnal haemoglobinuria**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The words **"no itching"** and **family history** are the discriminators from allergic angioedema. Note C1 **inhibitor** vs C1, C2, C4 **deficiency** — different answers, adjacent options.

**REMEMBER:** *No itch means no histamine — think bradykinin.*

`MC2616`

---

## 141. Graft Rejection and Hypersensitivity Types

**PEARL:** **Hyperacute rejection (minutes to hours) = TYPE II hypersensitivity**, mediated by **preformed antidonor antibodies**. **Acute and chronic rejection are largely type IV (T-cell mediated).**

**UNDERSTAND:** The **timing tells you the mechanism**. Minutes are far too short for a cellular response to be generated — only **antibody already in the circulation** can act that fast, binding endothelium, fixing complement and thrombosing the graft vessels immediately on reperfusion. T-cell responses require days to weeks of priming and expansion.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Hyperacute:** graft becomes **cyanotic, mottled**, with **neutrophil accumulation, fibrinoid necrosis and thrombotic occlusion**. Prevented by **ABO matching and cross-matching** — hence now rare.
- **Acute cellular rejection (days–weeks):** CD8 CTLs and CD4-driven inflammation → **tubulitis and interstitial mononuclear infiltrate**. **Acute humoral (antibody-mediated) rejection** → **C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries**.
- **Chronic rejection (months–years):** **fibrointimal proliferation and vascular narrowing** → chronic allograft nephropathy, **bronchiolitis obliterans** (lung), **cardiac allograft vasculopathy**.
- **Graft-versus-host disease** — donor T cells attacking the host — **skin, gut, liver**; risk in bone marrow transplant and non-irradiated blood products.
- Hypersensitivity revision: **I — IgE/mast cell (anaphylaxis, atopy)**; **II — antibody against cell-surface antigen (hyperacute rejection, ITP, Goodpasture, transfusion reaction)**; **III — immune complex (SLE, serum sickness, post-streptococcal GN)**; **IV — T-cell delayed (contact dermatitis, tuberculin test, acute graft rejection)**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Whenever a rejection question gives a **time frame**, map it to the mechanism first, then to the hypersensitivity number.

**REMEMBER:** *Minutes means antibody was already there — type II.*

`MA6841`

---

## 142. HHV-8 and the Oncoviruses

**PEARL:** **HHV-8 (KSHV)** causes **Kaposi sarcoma, primary effusion lymphoma, multicentric Castleman disease and KSHV inflammatory cytokine syndrome**.

**UNDERSTAND:** HHV-8 is a **gammaherpesvirus** that infects **endothelium and B lymphocytes** — which is exactly why its tumours are **vascular (Kaposi sarcoma)** and **lymphoid (effusion lymphoma, Castleman disease)**. It encodes viral homologues of **IL-6, cyclin D and Bcl-2**, driving proliferation and survival.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Kaposi sarcoma variants:** classic (elderly Mediterranean men), endemic African, iatrogenic (transplant), and **AIDS-related (an AIDS-defining illness)** — red-purple macules, plaques and nodules, often on the lower limb, palate and viscera.
- **Primary effusion lymphoma:** lymphomatous effusion **without a mass**, in body cavities, in HIV; often co-infected with **EBV**.
- Other human oncoviruses: **HBV and HCV → hepatocellular carcinoma**; **HPV 16/18 → cervical, anogenital, oropharyngeal cancer**; **EBV → Burkitt lymphoma, nasopharyngeal carcinoma, Hodgkin, post-transplant lymphoproliferative disease**; **HTLV-1 → adult T-cell leukaemia/lymphoma**; **Merkel cell polyomavirus → Merkel cell carcinoma**.
- Treatment of AIDS-related KS begins with **antiretroviral therapy**; chemotherapy (liposomal doxorubicin, paclitaxel) for extensive disease.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The image identifies the virus; the question asks for **another disease caused by the same virus**. Each distractor belongs to a different oncovirus — learn the mapping as pairs.

**REMEMBER:** *HHV-8 infects vessels and B cells, so it causes a vascular tumour and a lymphoma.*

`MA8280`

---

## 143. Immunoglobulin Classes

**PEARL:** **IgE is raised in chronic allergy and helminth infection.** It is the **only heat-labile** immunoglobulin, with the **shortest half-life (1–5 days)** and the **lowest serum concentration**.

**UNDERSTAND:** IgE is scarce in serum because it is **bound to FcεRI on mast cells and basophils**, where it acts as a fixed sensor rather than a circulating antibody. Cross-linking of that surface IgE by allergen triggers degranulation — the mechanism of **type I hypersensitivity**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **IgG** — most abundant, **half-life ~23 days (longest)**, **only Ig crossing the placenta**, **4 subclasses**, best at **opsonisation, complement fixation, toxin and virus neutralisation**; indicates **past/chronic** infection.
- **IgM** — **pentamer, largest**, half-life ~5 days, **intravascular**, first antibody in a primary response (**indicates recent infection**), **most efficient complement activator and agglutinator**; a **surface receptor** on naive B cells.
- **IgA** — **secretory dimer with J chain and secretory piece**, dominant in mucosal secretions, colostrum, tears, saliva; **most abundant immunoglobulin produced daily**.
- **IgD** — surface receptor on naive B cells with IgM; role in B cell activation. **IgM and IgD are co-expressed via alternative RNA splicing.**
- Immunoglobulin in the neonate: **maternal IgG (transplacental)** wanes by 3–6 months → **physiological hypogammaglobulinaemia**; **IgM in cord blood indicates intrauterine infection** (it cannot cross the placenta).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** One-liners on class properties. The most-asked facts: **IgG crosses the placenta**, **IgM appears first and is a pentamer**, **IgE is heat-labile and allergic**, **IgA is the secretory one**.

**REMEMBER:** *IgE is rare in serum because it is parked on mast cells.*

`MC7689`

---

## 144. Pertussis

**PEARL:** **Paroxysmal cough with an inspiratory whoop and post-tussive vomiting in an unimmunised child = pertussis** → treat with a **MACROLIDE (azithromycin, clarithromycin or erythromycin)**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Antibiotics **do not shorten the paroxysmal stage** once toxin-mediated airway damage is established — their purpose is to **eradicate the organism from the nasopharynx and stop transmission**. That is why treatment only alters the course if started **within the first 1–2 weeks (catarrhal stage)**, and why prophylaxis of contacts matters as much as treatment.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Bordetella pertussis** — Gram-negative coccobacillus, grown on **Bordet–Gengou or Regan–Lowe medium**, "**bisected pearl / mercury drop**" colonies. Diagnosis now by **PCR of nasopharyngeal swab**.
- Three stages: **catarrhal (most infectious, 1–2 weeks) → paroxysmal (2–6 weeks, whoop, post-tussive vomiting, subconjunctival haemorrhage) → convalescent** ("the 100-day cough").
- Blood picture: **marked absolute lymphocytosis** — driven by **pertussis toxin** blocking lymphocyte trafficking (it **ADP-ribosylates Gi**, raising cAMP).
- **Cotrimoxazole** is the alternative for macrolide-allergic patients. **Post-exposure prophylaxis with a macrolide** for household and high-risk contacts (infants, pregnant women, immunocompromised) regardless of immunisation status.
- **Azithromycin is preferred in infants < 1 month** — erythromycin is linked to **infantile hypertrophic pyloric stenosis**.
- Infants may present with **apnoea and no whoop at all** — the highest-mortality group.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Drug-of-choice recall, plus the classic epidemiological clue (unimmunised, contact 2–3 weeks earlier).

**REMEMBER:** *Antibiotics clear the carriage, not the cough.*

`MA2816`

---

## 145. Herpangina

**PEARL:** **Herpangina = grey-white papulovesicular ulcers on the SOFT PALATE, anterior tonsillar pillars, uvula and posterior oropharynx**, caused by **Coxsackie A — an ENTEROVIRUS**.

**UNDERSTAND:** The distribution is the diagnosis. Herpangina hits the **posterior** oral cavity (soft palate and pillars) because enteroviruses target lymphoid-rich oropharyngeal tissue; **primary herpetic gingivostomatitis** hits the **anterior** mouth — gingiva, tongue and lips — with **gingival inflammation**, which herpangina never causes.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Also caused by **Coxsackie B, echoviruses, enterovirus 71**; self-limiting in under a week; treatment is **supportive**.
- Same virus family causes **hand-foot-and-mouth disease** (Coxsackie **A16**, and **EV71 — associated with brainstem encephalitis and outbreaks**), **acute haemorrhagic conjunctivitis**, **myocarditis and pericarditis (Coxsackie B)**, **Bornholm disease/pleurodynia (Coxsackie B)**, and **aseptic meningitis** — enteroviruses are the **commonest cause of viral meningitis**.
- **Enteroviruses**: non-enveloped ssRNA Picornaviridae — **acid-stable**, hence they survive the stomach and spread **faeco-orally**; peak in summer/monsoon.
- **Poliovirus** is the other major enterovirus; wild poliovirus is now near-eradicated.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Image of posterior palatal ulcers. Distinguish **herpangina (posterior, enterovirus)** from **herpetic gingivostomatitis (anterior, gingivitis, HSV-1)**.

**REMEMBER:** *Back of the mouth is Coxsackie; front of the mouth is herpes.*

`MA7974`

---

## 146. Paul–Bunnell Test and the Named Serological Tests

**PEARL:** The **Paul–Bunnell test** is the **heterophile antibody test for infectious mononucleosis ("kissing disease", EBV)** — it agglutinates **sheep erythrocytes**, titre **≥ 1:100** being significant.

**UNDERSTAND:** EBV infection generates **heterophile antibodies** — antibodies that cross-react with antigens of unrelated species. They are not directed against EBV at all; they are a **by-product of polyclonal B-cell activation**, which is why the test is a *marker* of the disease rather than a specific assay. **Davidsohn differential absorption** distinguishes true IM heterophile antibodies from those of serum sickness and Forssman antigen.

**MUST KNOW:**
- IM: **fever, exudative pharyngitis, symmetrical lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly**, **atypical (Downey) lymphocytes**, and a **maculopapular rash after ampicillin/amoxicillin**. **Avoid contact sports** for weeks (splenic rupture).
- Specific serology: **anti-VCA IgM (acute), anti-VCA IgG, anti-EBNA (appears late — its presence excludes acute infection)**.
- Other named serological tests worth pairing: **Ascoli's thermoprecipitin test — anthrax**; **VDRL/RPR (flocculation) and TPHA/FTA-ABS — syphilis**; **Weil–Felix — rickettsiae (Proteus OX antigens)**; **Widal — enteric fever**; **Frei test — LGV**; **Casoni — hydatid**; **Montenegro — leishmaniasis**; **Lepromin — leprosy immune status**; **Elek gel precipitation — diphtheria toxigenicity**; **Quellung — capsular typing**.
- **rK39 immunochromatographic strip test** diagnoses **visceral leishmaniasis** — the answer when a traveller with **FUO** has a marrow showing **amastigotes with a kinetoplast (LD bodies, "double-dot")**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Eponym matching. "Kissing disease" is the deliberately colloquial way of asking for infectious mononucleosis.

**REMEMBER:** *Heterophile antibodies agglutinate sheep cells — nothing to do with EBV antigens.*

`MA8272, MF0660`

---

## 147. Viridans Streptococci — Who Does What

**PEARL:** **Streptococcus mutans causes dental caries** by converting dietary **sucrose into acid and adhesive dextran (glucan)**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Two products, two effects. The **acid demineralises enamel and dentine**; the **sticky dextran glues bacteria, food debris and epithelial cells into plaque**, holding that acid against the tooth surface. Neither alone would cause caries — it is the combination of an acid producer that can also **cement itself in place** that makes *S. mutans* uniquely cariogenic.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Viridans group by niche:** ***S. mutans* — dental caries**; ***S. sanguis* — subacute bacterial endocarditis** (on previously damaged valves, after dental procedures); ***S. mitis*, *S. salivarius*** — oral commensals; ***S. anginosus/milleri* group — abscesses** (brain, liver, dental).
- Viridans vs pneumococcus: **viridans are non-capsulated, optochin-RESISTANT, bile-INSOLUBLE, inulin-negative, dome-shaped colonies, granular turbidity in broth**; pneumococcus is the opposite on every count (capsulated, optochin-sensitive, bile-soluble, inulin-positive, draughtsman colonies, uniform turbidity).
- ***Streptococcus gallolyticus* (formerly *S. bovis*)** — **group D, non-enterococcal**; bacteraemia and endocarditis with it demands **colonoscopy** to look for **colorectal carcinoma or polyps**. It is **bile-insoluble, esculin-hydrolysis positive, PYR-NEGATIVE, and does NOT grow in 6.5% NaCl** — the last two distinguish it from **enterococci (PYR-positive, salt-tolerant)**.
- **Enterococcus faecium** is the classic **vancomycin-resistant (VRE)** organism — suspect it when **catalase-negative Gram-positive cocci in chains** persist in blood cultures **despite two weeks of vancomycin**, typically from a **central line** in an ICU patient on broad-spectrum antibiotics. Treat with **linezolid or daptomycin**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Species-level questions. Anchor **mutans → caries**, **sanguis → endocarditis**, **gallolyticus → colon cancer**, **faecium → vancomycin resistance**.

**REMEMBER:** *Mutans makes glue and acid; gallolyticus points at the colon.*

`MA1570, MA1587, MA1572`

---

## 148. Streptococcal Antigens That Cross-React with Human Tissue

**PEARL:** **M protein cross-reacts with MYOCARDIUM** (acute rheumatic fever). **Group-specific C carbohydrate cross-reacts with cardiac VALVES.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Rheumatic fever is **molecular mimicry** — antibodies raised against streptococcal surface antigens happen to fit human proteins with similar epitopes. Each streptococcal component mimics a different human tissue, which is why one organism produces a **multi-organ** post-infectious disease (heart, joints, skin, brain) rather than one lesion.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Map: **M protein → myocardium (ARF)**; **C carbohydrate → cardiac valves (ARF)**; **hyaluronic acid capsule → synovial fluid (arthritis)**; **peptidoglycan → skin (guttate psoriasis)**; **cytoplasmic membrane → glomerular basement membrane/vascular intima (acute glomerulonephritis)**; **streptococcal antigens → caudate/subthalamic neurons (Sydenham chorea)**.
- **M protein is the major virulence factor** — antiphagocytic, and the basis of **type-specific immunity** (over 100 types, hence repeated infections).
- **ARF follows pharyngitis only; acute glomerulonephritis follows pharyngitis OR skin infection** — and treating the sore throat prevents ARF but does not reliably prevent AGN.
- **Two haemolysins:** **Streptolysin O** — **O**xygen-labile, heat-labile, **antigenic (basis of the ASO titre)**, cardiotoxic; **Streptolysin S** — **S**erum-soluble, oxygen-**stable**, **non-antigenic**, and **responsible for the zone of β-haemolysis seen on the blood agar plate** (a surface plate is exposed to oxygen, so streptolysin O is inactivated there).
- **Streptococcal pyrogenic exotoxins (erythrogenic toxins, of scarlet fever, detected by the Dick test): types A and C are BACTERIOPHAGE-mediated; type B is chromosomal.** Phage-coded toxins mnemonic **ABCDE**: group **A** strep SpeA/SpeC, **B**otulinum (C and D), **C**holera, **D**iphtheria, **E**HEC verocytotoxin.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Straight matching, and the M-protein/C-carbohydrate pair is the one most often swapped. Remember **M for Myocardium**.

**REMEMBER:** *M for myocardium, C for the cardiac valve Cusps.*

`MA1560, MA1556, MC1746`

---

## 149. Moist Heat — The Temperature-Time Pairs

**PEARL:** **Tyndallisation = 100 °C for 20 minutes on 3 successive days.** **Pasteurisation (holder) = 63 °C for 30 minutes**; **flash = 72 °C for 15–20 seconds**. **Autoclave = 121 °C, 15 psi, 15 minutes.** **Inspissation = 80–85 °C for 30 minutes on 3 days.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Intermittent methods exist to kill **spores without destroying heat-labile material**. A single exposure at 100 °C kills vegetative cells but not spores; the **intervening 24 hours let surviving spores germinate into vegetative forms**, which the next day's heating destroys. Three cycles therefore achieve sterility at a temperature that would otherwise be insufficient.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Tyndallisation** is used for media containing **gelatin, egg, serum or sugar**; it may still fail against **spores of some anaerobes and thermophiles** — hence autoclaving replaced it.
- **Inspissation** is the same principle at lower temperature, used to **solidify and sterilise Löwenstein–Jensen and Lowenstein media** (egg-based).
- **Pasteurisation is NOT sterilisation** — it kills non-sporing pathogens (**Mycobacterium, Brucella, Salmonella**) but **Coxiella burnetii may survive the holder method**. Milk is then **cooled rapidly to ≤ 13 °C**.
- **Autoclave (moist heat under pressure)** is the most reliable: **121 °C/15 psi/15 min**, or **134 °C/30 psi/3 min** for prions-related protocols. Control: **Bacillus stearothermophilus spore strips**, Browne's tube, Bowie–Dick tape.
- **Hot air oven (dry heat)**: **160 °C for 2 hours** — glassware, oils, powders; control organism **Bacillus subtilis subsp. niger**.
- **Louis Pasteur** — pasteurisation, germ theory, disproving spontaneous generation, rabies and fowl cholera vaccines; the **father of modern microbiology**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Pure numbers, and the options differ by a single digit or unit. Learn **63/30 min** and **72/15 sec** as a pair.

**REMEMBER:** *Heat, let the spores wake up, heat again.*

`MA6196, MF5706`

---

## 150. Microscopy — Matching Method to Organism

**PEARL:** **Dark-field microscopy is the method for demonstrating *Treponema pallidum*** in a chancre or condyloma lata — the organism is **too thin to be seen on Gram stain** and does not take up ordinary dyes.

**UNDERSTAND:** Dark-ground illumination lights the specimen **obliquely**, so only light **scattered by the object** reaches the objective — producing a **bright, refractile organism against a black background**. This makes visible objects **below the resolution limit of bright-field staining**, which is exactly the problem with a 0.2 µm-wide spirochaete.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Dark-field** — *T. pallidum* (motile corkscrew), Leptospira, Borrelia. Requires a **live, fresh specimen from a moist lesion**; useless on oral lesions (commensal treponemes).
- **Phase contrast** — internal structures of **living unstained** cells; dark image on a light background.
- **Fluorescence** — **auramine–rhodamine** for mycobacteria, **acridine orange**, malaria parasites, immunofluorescence assays.
- **Electron microscopy** (Ernst Ruska) — **viral morphology**, highest resolving power.
- **The bacterial cell wall CANNOT be seen with simple staining** or ordinary light microscopy. It is demonstrated by **plasmolysis, microdissection, mechanical rupture, reaction with specific antibody, differential staining (Chance's method), electron microscopy and fluorescence microscopy**.
- **Arrangement identifies the Gram-positive cocci:** **Staphylococcus — clusters**; **Streptococcus — chains**; **Pneumococcus — lanceolate pairs**; **Micrococcus — tetrads and packets of eight**. **Micrococci are catalase-positive and MODIFIED-oxidase positive**, rarely pathogenic, and separated from staphylococci by the **Hugh–Leifson test (micrococci oxidative, staphylococci fermentative)**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Method used to demonstrate the causative agent" with a painless ulcer → **dark-field**. Separately, "which will NOT visualise the cell wall" → **simple staining**.

**REMEMBER:** *Light it from the side to see what a stain cannot show.*

`ME9924, MA6188, MC1749`

---

## 151. Staphylococcus aureus — Colony Characteristics

**PEARL:** *S. aureus* pigment (**staphyloxanthin**, a **carotenoid**) is produced **optimally at 22 °C aerobically**, not at 35–37 °C. On a nutrient agar slope, confluent growth gives the **"oil-paint appearance."**

**UNDERSTAND:** Pigment synthesis is **temperature-dependent and requires oxygen** — which is why plates incubated at body temperature look pale and must be **left at room temperature** to develop the golden colour. Staphyloxanthin is not decorative: it is an **antioxidant virulence factor** that quenches neutrophil-derived reactive oxygen species.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Pigment production is **enhanced by adding 1% glycerol or milk** to the medium.
- Colonies: **2–4 mm, convex, smooth, shiny, opaque, easily emulsifiable, golden-yellow**; **β-haemolytic** on blood agar.
- Identification: **catalase-positive** (separates from streptococci), **coagulase-positive** (separates from CoNS), **mannitol fermenter on mannitol salt agar**, DNase positive, **PYR-negative**.
- **Selective media**: **mannitol salt agar, Ludlam's medium, salt milk agar** — all exploit **salt tolerance**.
- **MRSA control: hand hygiene before and after every patient contact is the single most effective measure.** Add contact precautions, **decolonisation with nasal mupirocin and chlorhexidine baths**, and exclusion of staff with active lesions from high-risk areas. **Empirical vancomycin for all patients and ward fumigation are wrong answers** — they drive resistance and are ineffective respectively.
- Toxins: **enterotoxins (heat-stable, superantigens — food poisoning within 1–6 h)**, **TSST-1**, **exfoliative toxin (SSSS)**, **Panton–Valentine leucocidin (necrotising pneumonia, recurrent abscesses)**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "False statement" format — the falsehood is the **temperature of optimal pigment production (22 °C, not 35 °C)**. The MRSA question tests infection control principles, where hand hygiene always wins.

**REMEMBER:** *Gold develops in the cold, on the bench, not in the incubator.*

`MA1501, MA1545, MD0633`

---

## 152. Neisseria gonorrhoeae — Facultative, Not Obligate

**PEARL:** *N. gonorrhoeae* is a **FACULTATIVE intracellular organism**, not an obligate one. It is **oxidase and catalase positive**, uses **type IV pili** as a virulence factor, and shows **twitching motility**.

**UNDERSTAND:** The Gram film shows it **inside neutrophils**, which invites the wrong conclusion that it must live there. In fact it survives perfectly well extracellularly — being taken up by polymorphs is part of the **host response**, and gonococci **resist killing inside them**. Contrast a true obligate intracellular organism like *Chlamydia*, which cannot make its own ATP.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Twitching motility** is **flagella-independent**, produced by **extension and retraction of type IV pili** across moist surfaces — the same pili mediate **attachment and inhibit phagocytosis**, and their **antigenic variation** is why there is no vaccine and no lasting immunity.
- Virulence factors: **pili, Opa (outer membrane) proteins, Por (PorB) protein, IgA1 protease, LOS, transferrin- and lactoferrin-binding proteins** (iron piracy).
- **Fastidious** — requires **chocolate agar/Thayer–Martin (VCN: vancomycin, colistin, nystatin) with 5–10% CO₂**; dies rapidly on transport, so use **Amies/Stuart transport medium** or direct plating.
- Complications: **PID, tubal infertility, ectopic pregnancy, epididymo-orchitis, disseminated gonococcal infection (pustular rash, tenosynovitis, migratory arthritis)** and **ophthalmia neonatorum**.
- Obligate intracellular organisms to keep separate: **Chlamydia, Rickettsia, Coxiella** (and viruses).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "All true EXCEPT" → **obligate intracellular**. The Gram-stain image is the bait; the biology is the answer.

**REMEMBER:** *It hides in neutrophils by choice, not by necessity.*

`MD0164`

---

## 153. Mollicutes

**PEARL:** **Mollicutes** = the class of bacteria **without a cell wall** — principal genera **Mycoplasma** and **Ureaplasma**; ***Ureaplasma urealyticum* causes non-gonococcal urethritis**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- No cell wall → **intrinsically resistant to all β-lactams** and **not seen on Gram stain**; treat with **macrolides, doxycycline or fluoroquinolones**.
- They are the **smallest free-living organisms**, need **cholesterol/sterols** in the medium, and grow as **"fried-egg" colonies**.
- ***M. pneumoniae*** — atypical pneumonia with **cold agglutinins**, bullous myringitis, and erythema multiforme/SJS.

**REMEMBER:** *No wall = no Gram stain, no penicillin. Fried-egg colonies.*

---

## 154. Listeria — Actin Comet Tails

**PEARL:** *Listeria monocytogenes* moves **within and between cells by polymerizing host actin filaments**, forming **"comet tails"** that propel it — allowing **cell-to-cell spread without ever entering the extracellular space** (so it escapes antibody).

**MUST KNOW:**
- Gram-positive rod with **tumbling motility at 22–25°C** and **cold enrichment** (grows at 4°C) — from unpasteurized dairy, soft cheese, cold meats.
- Affects **neonates, pregnant women, the elderly and the immunocompromised** — causing **meningitis** with a characteristic **CSF monocytosis**.
- **Treatment: ampicillin (+ gentamicin)** — it is **intrinsically resistant to cephalosporins**, which is why ampicillin is added to empirical meningitis regimens at the extremes of age.
- *Shigella* and *Rickettsia* use the same actin-tail trick.

**REMEMBER:** *Actin comet tail = travels cell to cell; cephalosporins never work — use ampicillin.*

---

## 155. Erysipelas — St. Anthony's Fire

**PEARL:** **St. Anthony's fire = erysipelas**, caused by **Group A β-haemolytic *Streptococcus pyogenes***.

**MUST KNOW:** Infection of the **upper dermis and superficial lymphatics** → a **raised, sharply demarcated, fiery-red plaque** (unlike cellulitis, which is deeper with **indistinct** margins), classically on the face or leg, with fever and lymphangitis. Treatment: **penicillin**. *S. pyogenes* is **bacitracin-sensitive, PYR-positive, with M protein as the key virulence factor**.

**REMEMBER:** *Sharp raised border = erysipelas = Group A Strep = penicillin.*

---

## 156. Bacterial Tracheitis

**PEARL:** Usually caused by ***Staphylococcus aureus***, typically **following a viral URI** — high fever, toxic appearance and severe respiratory distress. X-ray: **subglottic narrowing with IRREGULAR tracheal margins and membrane-like opacities**, unlike the **smooth "steeple sign"** of viral croup.

**MUST KNOW:** It is the **great mimic** — croup-like stridor but a toxic child who **does not respond to nebulized adrenaline/steroids**. Needs **airway protection (intubation is often required), bronchoscopic removal of membranes and IV antistaphylococcal antibiotics**. Unlike epiglottitis, there is a **brassy cough** and drooling is uncommon.

**REMEMBER:** *Toxic child + croupy cough + no response to adrenaline = bacterial tracheitis.*

---

## 157. Burkholderia cepacia — Resistance

**PEARL:** *B. cepacia* is resistant to many antibiotic classes, including **second-generation cephalosporins such as cefotetan**.

**MUST KNOW:** It is **intrinsically resistant to aminoglycosides and polymyxins/colistin** — a useful identification clue. Active drugs: **cotrimoxazole, meropenem, minocycline, ceftazidime**. It is a feared pathogen in **cystic fibrosis** (**cepacia syndrome**) and in **chronic granulomatous disease**, and famously contaminates disinfectants and nebulizer solutions.

**REMEMBER:** *Cepacia laughs at aminoglycosides and colistin — use cotrimoxazole/meropenem.*

---

## 158. GeneXpert (CBNAAT)

**PEARL:** GeneXpert detects **mutations in the rpoB gene** to identify **rifampicin resistance**.

**MUST KNOW:** It simultaneously detects *M. tuberculosis* DNA and RR in **under 2 hours** from sputum. **Rifampicin resistance is used as a surrogate marker for MDR-TB** because >90% of RIF-resistant strains are also INH-resistant. Under India's NTEP, **CBNAAT is the first diagnostic test** for presumptive TB in children, PLHIV, extrapulmonary and drug-resistant TB.

**REMEMBER:** *rpoB mutation = rifampicin resistance = presume MDR-TB.*

---

## 159. Cholera Vaccine (Dukoral)

**PEARL:** **Dukoral** targets ***Vibrio cholerae* O1** and induces **intestinal (mucosal) immunity**, preventing colonization and neutralizing cholera toxin.

**MUST KNOW:** It is an **oral, killed whole-cell vaccine with the recombinant B subunit (WC-rBS)**. Because the **cholera toxin B subunit resembles the heat-labile toxin of ETEC**, it also gives short-term protection against **traveller's diarrhoea**. **It does not cover O139** (Shanchol/Euvichol are the bivalent O1+O139 vaccines used in India).

**REMEMBER:** *Dukoral = killed cells + B subunit = O1 only, plus a bonus against ETEC.*

---

## 160. Lymphogranuloma Venereum

**PEARL:** Incubation period **10–30 days**. The **groove sign** — a depression created by the **inguinal ligament** running between enlarged, inflamed inguinal and femoral lymph node groups — is characteristic.

**MUST KNOW:**
- *Chlamydia trachomatis* serovars **L1, L2, L3**.
- Three stages: **(1)** transient painless papule/ulcer (often missed), **(2)** painful matted **buboes** that suppurate through multiple sinuses, **(3)** proctocolitis, fistulae, strictures and **elephantiasis (esthiomene)**.
- Treatment: **doxycycline 100 mg BD × 21 days**; **aspirate buboes — do not incise** (creates chronic sinuses).

**REMEMBER:** *Groove sign = LGV; aspirate the bubo, and treat for 21 days.*

---

## 161. CSF-VDRL

**PEARL:** The **CSF-VDRL is highly SPECIFIC — a reactive result is diagnostic of neurosyphilis.**

**UNDERSTAND:** But it is **insensitive** — a **negative CSF-VDRL does NOT rule out neurosyphilis**. That asymmetry is the whole exam point.

**MUST KNOW:** **CSF-FTA-ABS** is the reverse — sensitive but not specific, so a **negative FTA-ABS effectively excludes** neurosyphilis. Supportive CSF findings: **lymphocytic pleocytosis and raised protein**. Treatment: **IV aqueous crystalline penicillin G × 10–14 days** (not benzathine penicillin, which does not enter the CSF).

**REMEMBER:** *CSF-VDRL positive = neurosyphilis; negative = proves nothing.*

---

## 162. Saline vs Iodine Wet Mount

**PEARL:**
- **Saline wet mount** — shows the **MOTILITY of trophozoites** (*E. histolytica*, *Giardia lamblia*, *Balantidium coli*), does not dry as fast, and **differentiates bile-stained from non-bile-stained eggs**.
- **Iodine wet mount** — kills the organisms but **stains glycogen and nuclei**, confirming **CYSTS**: **pale refractile nuclei, brown glycogen, yellow-gold cytoplasm**.

**REMEMBER:** *Saline for moving trophozoites; iodine for identifying cysts.*

---

## 163. Trichophyton — Macroconidia

**PEARL:** **Pencil-shaped (cigar-shaped), smooth, thin-walled macroconidia** distinguish ***Trichophyton***; its abundant **microconidia** are the more prominent feature.

**MUST KNOW:** Compare — ***Microsporum***: **spindle-shaped, rough (echinulate), thick-walled** macroconidia, few microconidia; ***Epidermophyton***: **club-shaped macroconidia in clusters and NO microconidia**. *Trichophyton* infects **skin, hair and nails**; *Epidermophyton* spares hair; *Microsporum* spares nails. **KOH mount** for direct demonstration; **Sabouraud dextrose agar** for culture.

**REMEMBER:** *Trichophyton = pencil; Microsporum = spindle with spikes; Epidermophyton = clubs, no microconidia.*

---

## 164. Coccidioidomycosis

**PEARL:** **"Valley fever" / "desert rheumatism"**, caused by ***Coccidioides immitis* or *C. posadasii***.

**MUST KNOW:** Endemic to the **arid south-western USA/Mexico**; inhalation of **arthroconidia** from dust. Tissue form = the diagnostic **thick-walled spherule packed with endospores** (not a yeast). Most infections are self-limiting flu-like illness ± **erythema nodosum**; **dissemination is far commoner in pregnancy, and in Filipino and African-American patients**, with a predilection for **meninges, bone and skin**. Treatment: **fluconazole/itraconazole**; amphotericin B for severe disease.

**REMEMBER:** *Desert dust + spherule with endospores = Coccidioides.*

---

## 165. Scrub Typhus — Vector

**PEARL:** The **trombiculid mite**, specifically its **larval stage (chigger)**, transmits ***Orientia tsutsugamushi*** — leaving a characteristic **black eschar** at the bite site.

**MUST KNOW:**
- The mite is both the **vector AND the reservoir** (**transovarial transmission**); humans are accidental hosts in the "**mite islands**" of scrub vegetation.
- Fever, headache, myalgia, **generalized lymphadenopathy** and eschar; complications — pneumonitis, ARDS, meningoencephalitis, AKI.
- **Weil–Felix (OX-K)** is the classic (insensitive) serology; **IgM ELISA** is now used.
- **Doxycycline** is the drug of choice (**azithromycin** in pregnancy/children).

**REMEMBER:** *Chigger bite → eschar → doxycycline. Mite is vector and reservoir.*

---

## 166. Hepatitis A and Solvent/Detergent Plasma

**PEARL:** **HAV is a NON-ENVELOPED virus, so it is not inactivated by solvent/detergent (SD) treatment of plasma.**

**UNDERSTAND:** SD treatment works by **dissolving lipid envelopes** — so it reliably kills **HIV, HBV, HCV** (enveloped) but leaves **non-enveloped viruses (HAV, parvovirus B19)** infectious. This is exactly why SD plasma still carries a residual HAV/B19 risk.

**MUST KNOW:** Non-enveloped viruses are also more resistant to **alcohol, drying, detergents, acid and bile** — hence HAV's faecal-oral spread.

**REMEMBER:** *No envelope = nothing for the solvent to dissolve.*

---

## 167. Nude Mice

**PEARL:** **Nude mice lack a thymus** → no mature T cells → they **cannot reject xenografts** — demonstrating that **T cells drive graft rejection**.

**MUST KNOW:** They are hairless and athymic (**FOXN1** mutation), retain **B cells and NK cells**, and are used to grow **human tumour xenografts**. The human counterpart is **DiGeorge syndrome** (thymic aplasia, 22q11 deletion, hypocalcaemia, cardiac anomalies) — where **live vaccines are contraindicated**. **SCID mice** lack both T and B cells.

**REMEMBER:** *No thymus → no T cells → no rejection.*

---
